# Requirements

Permanent ids. Format and rules: `docs/FLOW.md`. Rows are written at spec time for each
milestone; a changed requirement gets a new id and the old row says `replaced by FR-###`.

M15 review acceptance (Task 09, 2026-11-20). Replay FR-509 with the exact `origin/main` diplomat
from `79a94b2`. A Council-support purchase may prioritize one unit of an ordinary FR-245 raw bid
when the public supplier and buyer order directs it to the intended minor (FR-497). The mill
shortfall, price, freight, cash reserve and final-two-turn limits still apply.
FR-517's Armoury income is the number of horses freight will deliver under the current allocation.
Explain each changed FR-518 upkeep schedule beside its pin. Keep the default browser timeout,
save compatibility, the FR-125 minimum ratio of 1.5 and the original two-islands golden.

**Owner decisions, 2026-11-20.** Q140 confines advance hull-material preparation to nations with
`controller === "scripted"`. A human-controlled nation using the same planner may still place an
already-funded ship order. Q141 opens Council purchase priority one turn earlier, on the meeting
before the last scheduled Council. FR-245 still controls the mill shortfall, affordability and
final-two-turn cutoff. Compare the same 24 idle seeds before replacing FR-542(ii). FR-542(i)
keeps its original 18/24 embassy-and-pact requirement.

Task 19 gate (2026-11-22). All functional assertions run in `npm test`, including FR-497's private-information and rejected-order checks. Run all measured wall-clock limits (FR-481 and FR-497) under `npm run test:perf` alone, with one worker after the other gates. Preserve all thresholds, the 30-second engine lint cases and FR-109's existing browser timeout.

Status values: `planned`, `tested`, `partial`, `parked`, `replaced by FR-###`, `dropped`; project-level rows (NR) may also say `in force`.
A status starts with its value; the text after it is detail. A row whose wording a later row replaced starts with
`replaced by FR-###` (`in part (...)` when the rest holds), so the old row names the row that replaced it; a
replacing row does not always name the old row back (M12 review: 19 do not).

What `tested` means: a test checks that the code does what the row says, and that test passes in the suite. It does
not mean the row equals the 1997 game: a row marked `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` or built on a guessed number stays a guess (FR-199's regiment costs are
an example), and a row that holds only on pinned seeds or in a test-only world says so in its status (FR-213, FR-274,
FR-073, FR-074, FR-078).
`partial` means a test or check covers part of the row, and the status names what has none (an e2e case never written, a clause
the code does not meet, a document row checked only by a script outside the suite). `parked` means the row is built in part or not at all, and
the status says why and which milestone or decision closes it. A row with no passing test that can be found is `partial` or `parked`, never
`tested` (M12 close-out, 2026-11-16).

Unused ids: FR-143 (dropped before M5) and FR-282 to FR-289 (never written; M10 starts at FR-290).

## M1 Headless core

Written 2026-11-03 at the start of M1 part A (engine). Part B (store and UI wiring) adds its
own rows after FR-017.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-001 | The game engine lives in `src/app/engine/` and imports no React, Zustand, Next, `@/store` or `@/components`, and uses no DOM globals or `Math.random`/`Date.now`; an ESLint rule enforces this. | tested |
| FR-002 | `GameState` carries `seed: number`. WHEN a turn resolves THEN every random draw comes from an RNG derived only from the seed and the turn number, so no random state lives outside `GameState`. | tested |
| FR-003 | Every action the UI offers today is an `Order`, a union discriminated on `kind`. Every order names its `nationId` and its worker or tile ids explicitly; no order starts more than one worker. | tested |
| FR-004 | WHEN an order is validated THEN `validateOrder(state, order)` returns `{ ok: true }` or `{ ok: false, reason }` with a reason from a fixed string-literal union, and never changes the state. | tested |
| FR-005 | WHEN a valid order is applied THEN `applyOrder` performs its immediate effects (move the worker, start the job, charge the treasury or warehouse). WHEN the order is invalid THEN it returns the same state object unchanged. | tested |
| FR-006 | WHEN a worker order names a worker that is working THEN it is refused with `worker-busy`; WHEN the worker already moved this turn THEN it is refused with `worker-moved`. This holds for move, move-and-start, start and engineer rail-on-move. | tested |
| FR-007 | Move-and-start is all or nothing: WHEN the job cannot start on the target THEN neither the move nor the charge happens and the reason is returned (`insufficient-funds` when money is short); there is no retry with an unlimited treasury. | tested |
| FR-008 | WHEN an engineer moves onto an adjacent tile to build rail THEN the order is refused unless the engineer is available and unmoved, the tile is a valid rail site with no unfinished construction job (`tile-occupied`), and the nation can pay. | replaced by FR-113 and FR-137 in part (its `tile-occupied` clause and its rail-site clause; the rest holds); tested; its `tile-occupied` clause replaced by FR-113, its rail-site clause by FR-137 |
| FR-009 | WHEN a port is started (chosen or picked automatically) THEN the tile must have a river or an adjacent ocean tile, otherwise `invalid-site`. | tested |
| FR-010 | Transport orders: WHEN allocations are set THEN every amount is a non-negative number; WHEN capacity is bought THEN one coal and one iron ore per unit are taken from the warehouse and the increase is queued for next turn, all or nothing; WHEN a queued increase is reduced THEN the materials are refunded. | replaced by FR-039 (freight cars cost lumber, steel and labour) |
| FR-011 | `resolveTurn(state, ordersByNation, seed)` returns `{ state, events }`. It is the only place where the turn's orders are applied (nations in state order), the queued transport capacity is applied, all systems run, workers are reset and the turn and year advance. A rejected order produces an `order-rejected` event with its reason. | tested |
| FR-012 | Every turn system has the shape `(state, ctx) => state`, where `ctx` carries the RNG, the turn's accepted orders and an events sink. | tested |
| FR-013 | No engine function mutates its input: WHEN the input state is deep-frozen THEN orders, systems (the rail network helpers included) and `resolveTurn` run without error and the input stays deep-equal to its snapshot. | tested |
| FR-014 | Replay determinism: WHEN the same initial state and the same order list run for 10 turns twice THEN the final states are deep-equal. | tested |
| FR-015 | Two nations from `mockNation.ts` play 20 turns headlessly, each through the same order API, driven by a scripted player. Both treasuries change and after every turn no worker is on two tiles, no treasury is negative and no job is orphaned. | tested |
| FR-016 | A scripted player is a function `(state, nationId) => Order[]` in `engine/ai/scriptedPlayer.ts`; it only proposes orders that `validateOrder` accepts in sequence. | tested |
| FR-017 | Until part B wires the store to orders, the store's worker actions (`workerHelpers`, `handleEngineerMovement`, `startEngineerWork`, the worker start functions) are thin adapters over `validateOrder`/`applyOrder`, so FR-006 to FR-009 hold in the running app. | replaced by FR-022, FR-023 (part B removed the adapters) |

### M1 part B: store and UI on the order API

Written 2026-11-03 at the start of M1 part B.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-018 | The store holds exactly one engine `GameState` under `game` and keeps UI and session state beside it (camera, overlays, selection, modal flags, the active nation, the last refused order, the event log). `GameState` carries no UI fields: `activeNationId`, `newsLog`, `turnOrder` and the worker's `jobDescription` are gone. Only `game` is ever passed to the engine. | tested |
| FR-019 | WHEN a new game starts THEN it gets a seed, stored in `game.seed`: the one given, or else one drawn at random. Test fixtures use seed 1. | tested |
| FR-020 | WHEN the UI submits an order THEN `submitOrder(order)` validates it against `game` with the engine and returns the result: a valid order replaces `game` with the engine's result and clears the last refusal; a refused order leaves `game` untouched and keeps `{ order, reason }` in UI state; an order for a nation other than the active one is refused with `not-owner`. | tested |
| FR-021 | Orders take effect when submitted. WHEN the turn ends THEN `advanceTurn` calls `resolveTurn(game, {}, game.seed)`, stores the returned state, appends `{ turn, year, events }` to the event log (the last 20 turns are kept) and clears the last refusal. | replaced by FR-099 in part (the empty `{}` for the computer nations' orders; the rest holds); tested; the `{}` replaced by FR-099 (the computer nations' orders) |
| FR-022 | Every UI action that changes the game is an order sent through `submitOrder`: tile clicks, the tile panel's start and cancel buttons, the construction dialog, the capital's capacity slider and the transport allocation sliders. No component or hook writes game state or calls the engine's apply or resolve functions. | tested |
| FR-023 | A tile click with a worker selected tries its orders in turn and stops at the first one accepted or at `insufficient-funds`: a job on another tile is tried as move-and-start, then as a plain move (pre-M1 behaviour); an engineer moving next to its rail network tries rail-on-move first, then the plain move; an engineer standing on a rail node and stepping onto a neighbouring rail node of its nation that no section joins to his tile tries that section first (`moveAndStartConstruction`, structure `rail`, `fromTileId` = his tile; FR-137), then the plain move. Every order carries the active nation's id. | replaced by FR-461 in part (rail-on-move is the first order of an engineer's click on a neighbouring tile, the capital included, only where the engine's `railOnMoveStart` finds a section into it; where it finds none (the rail already reaches the tile, or the terrain refuses rail) the click is a plain move unless the rail-node-to-rail-node clause applies; the rest holds); tested; the rail-node-to-rail-node clause added in the M4.3 review fix round (two networks meeting head on could not be joined by a click) |
| FR-024 | The worker's job text is derived for display from the tile's job and the worker's status (`workerJobLabel`: "Mining to level 1", "Constructing depot", "Prospecting", "Moved"). The HUD shows the last turn's events and the last refused order in plain words. | tested |
| FR-025 | In the running app a player can, three turns in a row, select a worker, move it and start a job, and end the turn, with no console errors (`e2e/play-turns.spec.ts`). Playwright reads its port from `PORT` (default 3000) and starts this checkout's dev server unless `PW_REUSE_SERVER=1`. | tested |

### M1 review fix round

Written 2026-11-03 after the M1 review.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-026 | Orders and data keys are validated against own keys only (untrusted JSON from replay logs and AI players): a name that exists only on `Object.prototype` (`toString`, `constructor`, `__proto__`) is refused as an order target and never written into a nation's warehouse. | tested |

## M2 Economic loop

Written 2026-11-03 at the start of M2 part A (engine). Sources: `docs/rules/01` to `05`; open
numbers reference `docs/rules/gaps.md`. Part B (UI) adds its own rows after these.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-027 | Calendar: a new game starts on turn 1 in Spring 1815. Each turn is one season (Spring, Summer, Fall, Winter, in that order) and the year advances after Winter. `seasonOf(turn)` gives the season. | tested |
| FR-028 | Job durations come from one table (`definisions/workerDurations.ts`): prospecting 1 turn, rail 1, depot 3, port 3, fort 3, every development level 3 for every worker type. `constants/gameConstants.ts` holds no durations. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md A4: level-ups other than the miner's and the forester's first are not documented; default 3. | tested |
| FR-029 | Job costs, charged when the job is ordered: development to level 1, 2, 3 costs $100, $1,000, $5,000; depot $2,000; port $3,000; fort level 1 $5,000; rail by the terrain of the tile it is built on: $100 on farm, plains, desert and other flat land, $150 on tundra and either forest, $200 on hills, $300 on swamp. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md B3: rail on mountains is not documented, default $300; gaps.md A4: prospecting is not documented, default $100. | tested |
| FR-030 | Construction sites (refused with `invalid-site`): rail only where `canBuildRailAt` allows (also for `startConstruction`), and not on hills (barren or fertile) before Compound Steam Engine, swamp before Iron Railroad Bridge, mountains before Dynamite; a depot not on a tile with a depot and not next to a depot, a port or the capital (gaps.md F6: "not adjacent"), and not on hills before Compound Steam Engine; a port not where a port already is and not on hills or mountains; a fort only where the fort level is 0 (the capital counts as level 1; levels 2 and 3 need technology). A technology counts when `technologyState.technologies` holds it with `purchased: true`. | replaced by FR-137 in part (its rail clause; the rest holds); tested; rail clause replaced by FR-137 (with `fromTileId` a section may end on a rail tile) |
| FR-031 | WHEN a job ordered this turn is cancelled THEN its cost is refunded (rules 03: orders can be cancelled until End Turn and the cash comes back). WHEN a job ordered on an earlier turn is cancelled THEN it stops without a refund. | tested |
| FR-032 | A finished job leaves its tile in the resolution that finishes it: the worker is available next turn, another job may start on the tile next turn, and the completion event is the only trace. Jobs carry no `completed` or `completedOnTurn` flags. The map's "done" mark reads the last turn's completion events. | tested |
| FR-033 | Every nation has the production buildings textile mill, lumber mill, steel mill (mills, start capacity 2), clothing factory, furniture factory, metal works (factories, start capacity 1), refinery (a mill, capacity 0 until built, only after Oil Drilling) and food processing (no capacity limit). Standing production orders are kept between turns, except food processing, whose orders are cleared after each production phase. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md A6: a new game starts with the six mills and factories built (the Introductory and Easy rule and the gaps.md fallback). | tested |
| FR-034 | Recipes per output unit (rules 05 section 2): fabric 2 cotton-or-wool (any mix); clothing 2 fabric; lumber or paper 2 timber (one shared capacity); furniture 2 lumber; steel 1 coal + 1 iron ore; hardware or armaments 2 steel (one shared capacity); fuel 2 oil; each takes 2 labour. Canned food is made in batches of 2 cans from 2 grain + 1 fruit + 1 livestock-or-fish + 2 labour. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md B6: a single can for one odd labour unit is not modelled; canned food orders are even. | tested |
| FR-035 | Labour: a nation's labour is untrained x1 + trained x2 + expert x4, not counting sick workers and workers in training. Standing production orders and the freight cars ordered this turn (2 each) commit labour. `labourOf(nation)` returns `{ total, committed, free }`. | replaced by FR-117 in part ("standing production orders commit labour"; the rest holds); tested; "standing orders commit labour" replaced by FR-117 |
| FR-036 | `setProduction { nationId, building, output?, amount }` sets the building's standing order for that output (default: the building's first output). Refused: an unknown or unbuilt building or an output it does not make (`invalid-target`); an amount that is not a non-negative integer, or odd for canned food (`invalid-amount`); an amount above the building's capacity less its other output's order (`limit-reached`); an amount whose inputs exceed the warehouse stock (`insufficient-resources`) or whose labour, with all other standing orders, exceeds the nation's labour (`insufficient-labour`). Lowering an order is always accepted. Applying it changes nothing but the order. | replaced by FR-118 in part (its stock and labour clauses; the rest holds); tested; its stock and labour clauses replaced by FR-118 |
| FR-037 | Production phase: WHEN a turn resolves THEN, for each nation, the freight cars ordered are built first and then each building in a fixed order produces min(order, capacity, what the warehouse inputs allow, what the remaining labour allows). Inputs and labour are taken as each runs; all outputs reach the warehouse after every building ran, so a product made this turn is not an input this turn. Each product made gives a `goods-produced` event. No warehouse stock ever becomes negative. | tested |
| FR-038 | `expandBuilding { nationId, building }` raises a capped building one step: mills and the refinery 0, 2, 4, 8, 16, 24, then +8; factories 0, 1, 2, 4, 8, 12, then +4 (0 to the first step is the first construction). It costs 1 lumber + 1 steel per capacity point added, taken when ordered, all or nothing (`insufficient-resources`), no labour. The new capacity counts from next turn (after this turn's production phase) and gives a `building-expanded` event. Food processing, and the refinery before Oil Drilling, are refused (`invalid-target`). | tested |
| FR-039 | Transport orders: allocations are non-negative numbers (as FR-010). `changeTransportCapacity` with `delta > 0` orders freight cars: 1 lumber + 1 steel per car taken from the warehouse at once and 2 labour per car committed, all or nothing (`insufficient-resources`, `insufficient-labour`); a negative delta gives back part of this turn's order with a refund. The cars are built in the production phase and give a `freight-cars-built` event; a car the labour no longer covers is not built and its materials are refunded. The built cars carry goods from the next turn (FR-101, NR-016; until M4 they counted for this turn's shipping). | replaced by FR-101 in part (its timing: a freight car carries goods from the next turn; the rest holds); tested (timing replaced by FR-101) |
| FR-040 | Food: WHEN a turn resolves, after shipments reach the warehouse, THEN every industrial worker eats 1 food. Workers are fed experts first, then trained, then untrained; worker slot i in that order prefers grain, fruit, grain, livestock-or-fish, repeating. First every slot eats its preferred raw food, then the unfed eat canned food, then any other raw food (the worker is sick: no labour next turn), and a worker with nothing starves and leaves the workforce. A `workers-fed` event reports how many ate well, fell sick and starved. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md B7: who goes hungry first is not documented; default the untrained (then trained, then experts) take the sick and starved places, and sickness lasts until the next meal. | tested |
| FR-041 | Training: `trainWorkers { nationId, from, delta }` trains untrained to trained for $100 + 1 paper each, or trained to expert for $1,000 + 2 paper each, paid when ordered (a negative delta cancels this turn's trainees with a refund). Refused: experts (`invalid-target`), more trainees than healthy workers of that grade (`invalid-amount`), not enough cash (`insufficient-funds`) or paper (`insufficient-resources`), or a trainee's labour that is not free (`insufficient-labour`). A trainee gives no labour this turn and is promoted at the end of the production phase (`workers-trained` event). | replaced by FR-058 (training needs 1 free labour, not the trainee's) |
| FR-042 | Migration: `recruitWorkers { nationId, delta }` takes 1 canned food + 1 clothing + 1 furniture per worker when ordered, no cash and no labour (a negative delta gives back this turn's order). The new untrained workers join after this turn's meal and eat from the next turn on (`workers-recruited` event). At most max(1, floor(provinces / 4)) per turn (`limit-reached`). [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md B8: the cap is unconfirmed and the remake has no provinces yet, so at least 1. | tested |
| FR-043 | The engine owns the world a new game needs: `engine/newGame.ts` imports nothing from `@/testing`. A new game is the 40x40 default map with one nation that has $10,000, 4 untrained, 2 trained and 1 expert worker, 15 freight cars, the six mills and factories built and a start warehouse (grain 20; fruit, livestock, timber, cotton, wool, coal, iron ore, lumber, steel and canned food 10 each). Test fixtures stay in `src/app/testing`. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md A6: the start warehouse is not documented. | replaced by FR-080 (the two-island map; the rest restated there) |
| FR-044 | Acceptance: in a headless game, grain from a farm next to the capital reaches the warehouse through transport and, after a food processing order, ends up as canned food; the same game without the farm's grain makes no canned food. | tested |
| FR-045 | Two scripted nations play 20 turns headlessly and both produce goods and feed their workers; the FR-015 invariants hold after every turn, and no warehouse stock or worker count is ever negative. | tested |

## M3 Money and score

Written 2026-11-03 at the start of M3 part A (engine). Sources: `docs/rules/06` (market, holds),
`docs/rules/01` (game length, score), `docs/rules/05` section 4 (training, migration); open numbers
reference `docs/rules/gaps.md` and the decision cards Q10 to Q15 in `docs/journal.md`. The market is
one per game and every nation trades on it, so M4's second nation needs no new market. Part B (UI)
adds its own rows after these.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-060 | World market: the game SHALL have one market (`GameState.market`) shared by every nation, holding one world price per tradeable commodity in whole dollars, this turn's sell offers and buy bids by nation, and the purchases on their way by nation. Tradeable: every warehouse commodity except raw food (grain, fruit, livestock, fish), gold and gems (rules 06 section 2). A new game and the test worlds SHALL start at the start prices: resources $100 except horses $300, materials $300 except canned food $100, goods $900 (rules 06 section 4). `marketOf(state)` returns the market, or the start market for a hand-built state without one. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md A1, decision Q13: the exact start prices are not documented. | tested |
| FR-061 | Selling: `sellCommodity { nationId, commodity, amount }` SHALL set this turn's sell offer of the commodity to `amount` units. The units leave the warehouse when ordered; a lower amount returns the difference (0 cancels), so an offer and its cancel leave the state as it was. Refused: a commodity that is not tradeable or not an own key (`invalid-target`), an amount that is not a whole number of at least 0 (`invalid-amount`), a commodity the nation bids on this turn (`invalid-target`; rules 06: no offer and bid on the same commodity), more units than the warehouse holds (`insufficient-resources`), or more units traded this turn than the nation's holds (`limit-reached`, FR-063). Lowering an offer SHALL always be accepted. | tested |
| FR-062 | Buying: `buyCommodity { nationId, commodity, amount }` SHALL set this turn's buy bid of the commodity to `amount` units, paid when ordered at this turn's world price (prices change only in the trade phase, so the price paid is the deal price); a lower amount refunds the difference (0 cancels). Refused: a commodity that is not tradeable or not an own key (`invalid-target`), a bad amount (`invalid-amount`), a commodity the nation offers this turn (`invalid-target`), a fifth commodity bid on in one turn (`limit-reached`; rules 06: at most 4 buy bids), more units traded than the holds (`limit-reached`), or not enough cash (`insufficient-funds`). Lowering a bid SHALL always be accepted. | tested |
| FR-063 | Trade capacity: every unit sold or bought SHALL take one cargo hold for the turn, and a nation SHALL trade at most `merchantMarine.holds` units a turn, sales and purchases together (rules 06 section 3; a new game has 4 holds). v1 has no Shipyard, so the holds do not grow. `tradeCapacityOf(state, nationId)` SHALL return `{ holds, used, free }`. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] decision Q14: whether v1 keeps this limit. | tested |
| FR-064 | Trade phase: WHEN a turn resolves THEN the trade phase (after diplomacy, before production) SHALL pay every nation's sales at this turn's world price (a `goods-sold` event per commodity with amount, price and income), record its purchases (a `goods-bought` event with amount, price and cost) and clear the turn's offers and bids. The bought units SHALL reach the warehouse with the transported goods, after production and before the workers' meal, so production can use them from the next turn (rules 06 section 1: usable next turn). Trade orders last one turn. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] decision Q15: in the rules they persist until changed. | tested |
| FR-065 | Price drift: WHEN the trade phase has settled the deals THEN every tradeable commodity's price SHALL move by a random drift between -3% and +3% (from the turn RNG, one draw per commodity every turn, in a fixed commodity order) plus a push of +0.5% per unit bought and -0.5% per unit sold by all nations together, the push capped at 3% either way; the result is rounded to whole dollars and kept between 50% and 250% of the commodity's start price. The same seed and orders SHALL give the same prices. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md A1, decision Q13: the original price formula is not documented. | tested |
| FR-066 | Recruitment and training cost money: training SHALL keep costing cash and paper as in FR-058 (unchanged, not duplicated). Recruiting a migrant SHALL keep costing 1 canned food + 1 clothing + 1 furniture and no cash (FR-042, rules 05 section 4); all three are tradeable, so a nation without them SHALL be able to buy them on the world market in one turn and recruit on the next. Settled 2026-11-09 (decision Q10, NR-017): no cash fee. | tested |
| FR-067 | Score: `scoreOf(state, nationId)` SHALL be a pure query of the nation's score: treasury points floor(treasury / 100), industry points 10 per capacity point of its mills, factories and refinery (food processing has no capacity; capacity ordered this turn counts from the next turn), and development points 10 per resource level on its own tiles (a level-2 farm gives 20; a level-0 or undiscovered resource gives 0), returned with the parts and their total. `scoreTable(state)` SHALL list every nation's score, highest total first, ties in nation order. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md A7, decision Q11: the original weights are not documented. | replaced by FR-403 in part (M12, built: level 3 adds 50 points, so a level-3 tile is worth 70; tests/engine/score.test.ts); tested |
| FR-068 | Game over: a game SHALL last `turnLimit` turns, set by `newGame({ seed, turnLimit })` with default 40 (Spring 1815 to Winter 1824, just before the first Council meeting in Spring 1825); the test worlds also last 40; `turnLimitOf(state)` reads it (40 for a hand-built state without one). WHEN the turn numbered `turnLimit` resolves THEN, after the clock advances, the state SHALL hold `gameOver = { turn, year, scores }` (the last turn played, its year and the final `scoreTable`), and a `game-over` event SHALL follow the `turn-ended` event. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] decision Q12: the original game runs about 400 turns. | tested |
| FR-069 | After game over: `validateOrder` SHALL refuse every order with the new reason `game-over`, and `resolveTurn` SHALL be a no-op: it returns the same state object, runs no phase, leaves the clock as it is and reports each given order as `order-rejected` with reason `game-over`, so the final scores never change. | tested |
| FR-070 | Scripted player trades: each turn, after its production orders and before its worker orders, the scripted player SHALL (1) offer tradeable stock that neither this turn's production nor next turn's planned run needs (never canned food), with up to half its holds; (2) bid for the inputs that next turn's planned run lacks after this turn's production, with the holds left; (3) offer more surplus with any holds still free. The planned run is each built mill and factory, in production order, at capacity while the labour lasts after one canned-food batch and one freight car. To match that plan it SHALL plan production afresh each turn: clear its standing orders, order one canned-food batch first, then fill the buildings in production order (with bought coal and iron ore the kept mill orders took the labour the canned food needs, which broke FR-054). Every order it returns still validates in sequence (FR-016). | replaced by FR-128 (the holds) and FR-129 (the run, still planned afresh each turn) |
| FR-071 | Acceptance: two scripted nations SHALL play a 40-turn headless game in which both sell and buy on the world market, the FR-015 and FR-045 invariants hold after every turn and no order is refused; after turn 40 the game is over with a score for each nation, a 41st resolve changes nothing, and replaying the recorded orders from the same initial state gives a deep-equal final state with the same scores. | tested |
| FR-072 | News and refusal text: the news SHALL have a line for each new event ("Sold 2 Furniture for $1,800.", "Bought 3 Coal for $300.", "Game over at the end of Winter 1824.") and the refusal text SHALL name the new orders and reason ("Sale refused: the game is over."). The market dialog, the HUD score and the game-over screen are part B. | tested |

Part B (UI), written 2026-11-03 at the start of M3 part B. The UI builds orders and reads engine
queries only (FR-022); every number it shows comes from `marketOf`, `tradeOrdersOf`,
`tradeCapacityOf`, `scoreOf`, `turnLimitOf` and `state.gameOver`.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-073 | Market dialog: WHEN the player opens the market (the HUD's "Open Market" button or the capital's Trade button) THEN a dialog SHALL list every tradeable commodity in `TRADE_COMMODITIES` order with its display name, this turn's world price, its warehouse stock, and this turn's sell offer and buy bid; it SHALL show the cargo holds (used, free, total) and the buy bids used of `MAX_BUY_BIDS`. | tested in unit tests on the two-islands world; not reachable in the app (the app starts only the continent with the Board of Trade, so the "World market" dialog is a test-only fixture; card Q101, MKT-05); tested |
| FR-074 | Market dialog orders: WHEN the player presses a sell or buy "+" or "−" THEN the dialog SHALL send `sellCommodity` or `buyCommodity` through `submitOrder` with this turn's amount one higher or lower ("−" is disabled at 0); the engine judges every order. Opening the market SHALL clear the last refusal (as FR-055), and a refusal of an order sent from the dialog SHALL show in it in plain words ("Sale refused: not enough goods in the warehouse."). | tested in unit tests on the two-islands world; not reachable in the app (the "World market" dialog is a test-only fixture; card Q101, MKT-05); tested |
| FR-075 | HUD score and game length: the HUD SHALL show the active nation's score total (`scoreOf`) with its three parts on hover, and the turn as "Turn: N of L" with L from `turnLimitOf`. After game over it SHALL show "Game over" and the date of the last turn played instead, and no food warning (no turn follows). | tested |
| FR-076 | Game-over screen: WHEN `isGameOver(state)` THEN a game-over screen SHALL show the date of the last turn ("Winter 1824") and every nation's final score from `state.gameOver.scores`, ranked, each with treasury, industry and development points and the total, the active nation marked. The player MAY close it to look at the map and reopen it from the HUD ("Final scores"). The HUD's Advance Turn button SHALL be disabled after game over. | tested; M12 review round 1 (2026-11-16): the table adds an "In progress" column, FR-402's work in progress, so the parts add up to the total that ranks the nations (tests/ui/scoreAndGameOver.test.tsx, red before: no such column) |
| FR-077 | The store after game over: `advanceTurn` SHALL do nothing once the game is over: it does not call `resolveTurn`, adds no report to the event log and leaves `game` as the same object (so neither the button nor the "n" shortcut can log an empty turn). | tested |
| FR-078 | In the running app a player opens the market, offers one unit of a commodity for sale, ends the turn, and sees the sale in the news and the cash rise by the sale's income; and a player who ends 40 turns sees the game-over screen with the final scores and a disabled Advance Turn button, with no console errors (`e2e/market-and-game-over.spec.ts`). | tested in unit tests on the two-islands world; not reachable in the app (the running-app spec trades through the Board of Trade, FR-182; card Q101, MKT-05); tested; since M7 the app's continent trades with partners (FR-182), where nobody buys lumber, so the spec orders a consulate with Pellis and 1 furniture on turn 1, offers it on the Board of Trade on turn 2 and checks the cash rise by the income in the news ("Sold 1 Furniture to Pellis for $…"); the HUD reads "Cash: $10,000" since FR-258 |

## M3.5 Small map and clean look

Plan: `docs/REVIVAL_PLAN.md` M3.5 (NR-007 to NR-010). The rail fix comes first.

### Rail line fix

Bug report (Nathan, 2026-11-03): rail built from the capital west through a coast tile ended at the
tile before the coast on the map, yet the depot past the coast was green.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-079 | Rail line (NR-010): the map SHALL draw one line from tile centre to tile centre for every link of each nation's rail graph (`railLinksOf`), the graph `transportConnectivitySystem` lights the depots from, and nothing else; it SHALL NOT apply a terrain rule of its own. So rail built tile by tile on any tile the engine accepts for rail (`isRailSite`; coast tiles are land in v1) is drawn as one continuous line, every line joins two neighbouring tiles, and a depot is green only when the drawn lines join it to the capital (or to a port: the existing port rule, unchanged). A depot the rail does not reach is red and no line leads to it. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] decision Q16: whether coast tiles stay land (rail allowed) or become sea in M3.5. | tested; the graph holds only the sections built (FR-136) |

Q16 settled by the orchestrator (2026-11-03): the two-island map places no coast tiles; the shore is
the land tiles next to the sea, as in the original game. FR-079 is unchanged; its test now runs along
shore tiles of the new map (FR-080).

### Part A: two-island start world

Written 2026-11-03 at the start of M3.5 part A (NR-007). Sources: `docs/rules/02` sections 1 and 2
(capital on flat land with water access, farms and orchards next to it at level 1, terrain to
resource), `docs/rules/04` (ports next to the sea). Part B (faint tiles, icons, palette) adds its
own rows after these.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-080 | Two-island start world (NR-007; replaces FR-043, whose start nation is restated in FR-081): the engine owns the world a new game needs (`engine/newGame.ts` and every engine file import nothing from `@/testing`). A new game SHALL be a 24x16 map of sea with exactly two islands (two groups of land tiles joined by neighbour steps; no land tile of one touches the other), the same map for every seed. Each island SHALL hold the same six tile types: farm (grain), orchard (fruit), open range (livestock), plantation (cotton), hardwood forest (timber) and barren hills (coal or iron ore, hidden until prospected); every resource is at level 0 except next to the capital (FR-081). Island 2 SHALL be island 1 shifted 12 columns east. No other tile type is placed: no coast, river, town, mountain, swamp or desert tile (decision Q16 applied: the shore is the land next to the sea, and ports go there; the `Coast` and `River` terrain types stay in the code for the test worlds). | tested |
| FR-081 | The player's island: a new game SHALL have one nation, `nation-1`, with $10,000, 4 untrained, 2 trained and 1 expert worker, 15 freight cars, the six mills and factories built, the start warehouse (grain 20; fruit, livestock, timber, cotton, wool, coal, iron ore, lumber, steel and canned food 10 each) and the start technologies (FR-059). Its capital SHALL stand on a shore tile of island 1 (`CAPITAL_SITES[0]`) with the seven civilians on it and its rail network built, and the nation SHALL own every land tile of island 1 and no other tile. The capital's six neighbours SHALL be the sea, two farms, two orchards and one plantation, the land ones at level 1 (rules 02: farms and orchards next to the capital start at level 1; the 40x40 map's capital had the same five), so the first meal feeds all seven workers (FR-054). [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] gaps.md A6: the start warehouse is not documented. | replaced by FR-098 in part ("one nation": there are two; the rest holds); tested; "one nation" replaced by FR-098 (two nations) |
| FR-082 | Island 2 is left for M4's second nation: no tile of island 2 SHALL have an owner, a city or a worker. `CAPITAL_SITES[1]` SHALL be a flat shore tile of island 2 at the same place on its island as the player's capital, and `settleIsland(map, nationId, cityId, site)`, the function that places the player's capital, SHALL give a nation settled there a capital with the same neighbours at the same levels and the whole of island 2 (as many tiles of each type as island 1), and leave island 1 as it was. | replaced by FR-098 in part (the unclaimed island 2: nation 2 settles it; the rest holds); tested; "no tile of island 2 SHALL have an owner, a city or a worker" replaced by FR-098 (nation 2 settles it) |
| FR-083 | Camera on the small map: the camera SHALL be the map's scroll position, so it never shows space beyond the map's edges (edge scrolling stops at the map's edges, and the map's last column can scroll out from under the HUD). WHEN the map first shows, and WHEN the player presses the HUD's Center button, THEN the camera SHALL put the capital as near the middle of the map area left of the HUD as the map's edges allow. At the current 100 px tiles the 24x16 map (2,450 x 1,600 px) does not fit a 1280x720 screen next to the 250 px HUD; it fits with tiles of 42 px or less (part B, NR-008). Part B made the tiles 42 px: sizes restated in FR-088. | tested; the opening view replaced in part by FR-567 |

### Part B: icons and clean look

Written 2026-11-04 at the start of M3.5 part B (NR-008, NR-009). Decisions by Nathan and the
orchestrator, applied here: Game Icons (game-icons.net, CC BY 3.0) through `react-icons/gi`, faint
terrain tints with an icon for what the tile holds, a minimalist palette defined once, tiles small
enough for the whole map on 1280x720. Engine and game rules are unchanged; the cursors stay emoji
(an SVG data URI per action, `useTileVisuals`), out of scope here.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-084 | Icon set (NR-008): the UI's icons SHALL come from Game Icons through the `react-icons` package (`react-icons/gi`), pinned to an exact version in `package.json`. `components/icons.ts` SHALL map every `TerrainType`, every `ResourceType` and every `WorkerType` to a Game Icon (no type without one), and name the icons of the capital, depot, port, the three job markers and the HUD and dialog actions (warehouse, capital, market, transport, center, advance turn, final scores, about). Close buttons show a plain "×". | replaced by FR-318 (M11: the art pack's table gives terrains, resources and civilians their pictures) and FR-335 (the Game Icons that stay, `react-icons` still pinned; the per-type tables removed, tests/ui/icons.test.tsx) |
| FR-085 | What a tile shows: every land tile SHALL show one icon for what it holds (`tileIconOf`): the capital's or town's icon on a capital or town; else the icon of its resource when the resource is known (`discovered` is not `false`); else its terrain's icon (so barren hills show hills until prospected, then coal or iron ore). Open sea (`water`) SHALL show no icon. A resource at level 1 to 3 SHALL show a small badge "L1" to "L3"; level 0 shows none. Depots and ports show their icon with a green dot when connected and a red one when not (`data-connected`); job markers keep their titles ("Prospecting in progress", "Development in progress", "Construction in progress"); workers stay buttons (`data-worker-type`) showing their worker icon. No emoji SHALL be drawn on the map, in the HUD or in the dialogs (market, capital, warehouse, game over, construction, transport). | replaced by FR-322, FR-323, FR-324 and FR-325 in part (its tile icon, the depot and port icons and dots, and the worker icons; the rest holds); tested; its tile icon (`tileIconOf`) replaced by FR-322 and FR-323 (M11: the pack's picture, the mineral mark), the depot and port icons and dots by FR-324, the worker icons by FR-325; the level badge, the job markers' titles and the no-emoji rule kept (tests/ui/mapIcons.test.tsx, tests/ui/icons.test.tsx) |
| FR-086 | Palette (NR-009): the colours SHALL be defined once, as CSS variables in `styles/tokens.css`, and the map, HUD and dialogs SHALL use only those (no colour literal in `components/` or `hooks/`): light parchment land `--color-land`, pale blue sea `--color-sea`, a faint tint per terrain (`--terrain-<type>`, all light and low in saturation), one strong colour per nation (`--color-nation-1`, `--color-nation-2`) used only for territory borders, dark grey text and icons (`--color-ink`, `--color-ink-muted`), one accent colour for primary actions and the selection (`--color-accent`), panel surfaces (`--color-surface`, `--color-surface-sunken`), lines (`--color-line`), status colours (`--color-good`, `--color-bad`) and the job-completed glow (`--color-highlight`). `styles/theme.ts` names the variables for TypeScript (terrain tint and nation colour by nation order), and every variable it names SHALL be defined in `tokens.css`. | replaced by FR-097 and FR-321 in part ("nation colour by nation order" and the faint tint per terrain; the rest holds); tested; "nation colour by nation order" replaced by FR-097 (palette slot); the faint tint per terrain (`--terrain-<type>`, `TERRAIN_TINT`) replaced by FR-321 (M11: tiles lie on the map's ground; the 19 tokens and `TERRAIN_TINT` removed, Q71) |
| FR-087 | Ownership and selection (NR-009; fixes "selected and owned tiles are both red"): a nation's territory SHALL be drawn as border lines in the nation's colour along every tile side where an owned tile meets a tile of another owner, no owner or the map's edge, and on no side between two tiles of the same owner; a tile's own border is a faint grid line whatever its owner. The selected tile SHALL be drawn with an inset ring in the accent colour, a style no unselected tile has, owned or not. | replaced by FR-326 in part (the accent inset ring; the rest holds); tested; the accent inset ring replaced by FR-326 (M11: the pack's corner brackets, still on no unselected tile); the border lines and the grid line kept (FR-327, FR-321); QA fix round: the grid line on land only, the sea draws a transparent border (FR-355) |
| FR-088 | Tile size (NR-008; restates FR-083's sizes): tiles SHALL be 42 px (`TILE_PX` in `styles/theme.ts`, equal to `--tile-width` in `tokens.css`), odd rows shifted 21 px, and the rail and border overlays drawn on the same grid. The 24x16 map (1,029 x 672 px) SHALL fit a 1280x720 screen next to the 250 px HUD without scrolling: the camera stays at 0, 0 there. FR-083's camera rules are unchanged and still apply on a screen too small for the map. | tested |
| FR-089 | Credits (CC BY 3.0 attribution): the HUD SHALL have an About button that opens a small panel (`role="dialog"`, name "About") with the text "Icons by game-icons.net contributors, CC BY 3.0", a link to https://game-icons.net and a link to the licence (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/3.0/), and a close button. | tested |
| FR-090 | In the running app (`e2e/look.spec.ts`), on a 1280x720 screen a new game SHALL show the whole map left of the HUD without scrolling (the tiles 0-0 and 23-15 inside 1030 x 720, scroll position 0, 0), an icon on every land tile and none on sea, the capital's icon, the About panel's attribution, and a rail line built through the UI from the capital to a depot that shows connected; with no console errors. | replaced by FR-338 in part (its role as the look's check in the running app; the rest holds); tested (e2e/look.spec.ts, with the pack's pictures since M11); its role as the look's check in the running app replaced by FR-338 (e2e/m11-art.spec.ts) |

### Review fix round

Written 2026-11-04 after the M3.5 review (verdict "merge, nothing must-fix"); the six findings the
orchestrator chose to fix before the PR. Visuals, engine and `Nation.color` handling are unchanged.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-091 | Ready means scrolled (FR-083, FR-088): the map container's `data-ready="true"` SHALL appear only once the container's scroll position equals the first view's camera, so a reader that sees ready never sees the old 0, 0. The scroll SHALL be applied in the same commit as the camera (a layout effect), not in a later effect. | tested |
| FR-092 | Center always moves the map (FR-083): WHEN the player presses Center THEN the map container SHALL scroll to the camera on the capital, even when the stored camera already equals that camera (the stored camera can lag the real scroll position). | tested |
| FR-093 | Depot and port state without colour (FR-085): each depot and port SHALL be named "Depot: connected" / "Depot: not connected" ("Port: …" for ports) as its title and accessible name (`role="img"`), and SHALL show the state by shape as well as colour: a filled green dot when connected, a hollow red ring when not. `data-connected` stays. | replaced by FR-324 in part (the dots' drawing; the rest holds); tested; the dots' drawing replaced by FR-324 (M11: the pack's station marks, a filled disc and a ring, so the state still shows by shape); the names, `role="img"` and `data-connected` kept |
| FR-094 | Text contrast (WCAG 2.2 AA, 4.5:1 for normal text): in `styles/tokens.css`, `--color-good` and `--color-warn` SHALL reach at least 4.5:1 on `--color-surface`, `--color-good` also on `--color-surface-sunken`, and `--color-ink-muted` at least 4.5:1 on `--color-surface`, `--color-surface-sunken` and `--color-surface-raised`; a unit test computes the ratios from the token values. | tested |
| FR-095 | Tile names: every tile SHALL have an accessible name (`role="group"`, `aria-label`) built from its terrain's display name, its resource and level when the resource is known, and its owner, for example "Farm, grain, level 1, owned by Testland", "Barren hills, owned by Testland" (resource not yet prospected), "Orchard, fruit, level 0, not owned", "Sea". What the tile draws is unchanged. | tested |
| FR-096 | The browser tab SHALL be titled "Imperialism" (`metadata.title` in `layout.tsx`), not the template's "Create Next App". | tested |

## M4 Second nation

Written 2026-11-09 at the start of M4 part A (engine and store). Plan: `docs/REVIVAL_PLAN.md` M4; NR-006
(one player interface), NR-015 (start technologies), NR-016 (orders take effect when the turn ends).
Part B (the UI for watching nation 2) adds its own rows after these.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-097 | Nation colour (replaces FR-086's "nation colour by nation order"): each nation SHALL have a palette slot `colorSlot` (1, 2, …), its place in the founding order of `newGame()`, stored on the nation instead of a hex colour (the `color` field is gone). The colours SHALL live only in `styles/tokens.css` as `--color-nation-<slot>` (`--color-nation-1` crimson #9e2a5b, `--color-nation-2` blue #2c5c9e, values set by FR-110 (c)); `nationColor(slot)` in `styles/theme.ts` names the variable (a slot past the last token wraps round). The map's territory borders SHALL use the colour of the owner's slot, so a nation keeps its colour when `state.nations` is reordered, and no colour literal (such as #FF0000) SHALL remain in `src/app/engine`. | tested |
| FR-098 | Second nation (replaces the "one nation" of FR-081 and FR-082's unclaimed island 2): `newGame()` SHALL found two nations with one shared helper, `foundNation(map, founding)`, so both get the identical start kit: $10,000, 4 untrained, 2 trained and 1 expert worker, 15 freight cars, the six mills and factories, the start warehouse, the seven civilians on the capital, the capital's neighbours at level 1, the whole island and a rail network, plus the shared start technologies (FR-059, NR-015: rail on hills, swamp and mountains). Nation 1 is `nation-1` "Testland" with `capital-1` at `CAPITAL_SITES[0]`, slot 1; nation 2 is `nation-2` "Valdoria" (fictional) with `capital-2` at `CAPITAL_SITES[1]` (placed by `settleIsland`), slot 2. On island 2 a civilian of nation 2 SHALL be able to start developing at least one tile and its engineer to build rail next to the capital (both orders validate). | tested |
| FR-099 | Controller: each nation SHALL have `controller: "human" \| "scripted"` in the game state (a new game: nation 1 human, nation 2 scripted; the test fixtures' nations are human). `computerOrders(state)` (`engine/ai/players.ts`) SHALL return the orders of every nation whose controller is not human, from the controller-to-player registry `PLAYERS` (`scripted` → `scriptedPlayer`), in state order. At End Turn the store SHALL pass them to `resolveTurn` (it passed `{}`). The human's orders keep being applied when submitted (FR-020), and orders for a nation other than `activeNationId` are still refused (`not-owner`). | tested |
| FR-100 | Order log: the store SHALL keep `orderLog`. At End Turn it appends `{ turn, nationId, orders }` for every nation in state order: for the human nation the orders the store applied during the turn (accepted `submitOrder` calls, in order), for a computer nation the orders its player returned. Replaying the log through `resolveTurn` from the same start state SHALL give the same game. `startNewGame` SHALL empty it. | tested |
| FR-101 | Freight cars next turn (NR-016; replaces the same-turn use in FR-039): a freight car ordered this turn SHALL be built at the end of the turn as in FR-039 (1 lumber + 1 steel taken when ordered, 2 labour, built in the production phase while the labour lasts, the rest refunded, a `freight-cars-built` event) and SHALL carry goods only from the next turn: the production phase leaves the built cars in `transportCapacityPendingIncrease`, and the turn housekeeping (`promoteFreightCars`, after every phase) adds them to `transportCapacity`. This turn's shipping (logistics) and the food forecast use `transportCapacity` only (the scripted player reads no capacity: it orders one car a turn and ships by need); the capital dialog's freight-car control says "N ordered this turn, carrying goods from next turn". | tested |
| FR-102 | Growth acceptance: from the real `newGame()`, 10 turns resolved headless with nation 2's orders from `computerOrders` (the scripted player) and none for nation 1 SHALL leave nation 2 with more resource levels on its tiles than at the start, at least one rail link of its own (`railLinksOf`), and a higher score than at the start; no order of nation 2 is refused, and the same seed gives a deep-equal result. If the scripted player does not grow nation 2, it is improved (develop the nearest developable tile, build rail toward it), staying deterministic with every order valid in sequence (FR-016). | tested |
| FR-103 | Shared market: WHEN both nations sell the same commodity in one turn THEN the price SHALL move by the net units of both (the same price as one nation selling their sum, same seed), and each is paid at the turn's price. The 4-hold limit SHALL apply to each nation alone: nation 2 may trade 4 units when nation 1 has used its 4 holds. | tested |
| FR-104 | Both nations score: on the real new game `scoreTable` SHALL list `nation-1` and `nation-2` (equal start scores), and after the last turn `state.gameOver.scores` SHALL hold a score for each. | tested |
| FR-105 | Housekeeping from M3 and M3.5: (a) the unused `DEVELOPMENT_CONFIG` and `CONSTRUCTION_CONFIG` are removed; (b) `newGame({ turnLimit })` SHALL clamp the limit to a whole number from 1 to 400 (`TIME_CONFIG.MAX_TURN_LIMIT`; the original runs about 400 turns), and a value that is not a finite number gives the default 40; (c) the FR-070 test title in `twoNations.test.ts` names the coal, iron ore and timber bids it asserts; (d) the store's `startNewGame` SHALL start a new game and reset `isGameOverHidden`, the event log, the order log and the last refusal. | tested |

### Part B: watching the second nation

Written 2026-11-09 at the start of M4 part B (the UI). The human watches nation 2 (Valdoria, scripted)
grow and cannot act for it. FR-110 carries the M3.5 review items (2) to (5) and (7). The engine is
unchanged; the UI only builds orders and reads state and queries.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-106 | Score table: the HUD SHALL show the player's score (FR-075, unchanged) and a table of every nation ranked by score (`scoreTable`: highest first, ties in founding order) with, per row, the rank, a colour swatch in the nation's slot colour (`nationColor(colorSlot)`) beside the nation's name, "(you)" after the player's name, and the score total. The game-over screen SHALL list every nation with its rank and the same swatch beside its name. Colour SHALL never be the only identifier: the name is always shown as text and the swatch is hidden from assistive technology. (Extends FR-086: nation colours are used for the territory borders and these swatches.) Layout (cosmetic, not covered by a test; checked by eye): the HUD content SHALL scroll on its own while the compact action buttons stay fixed below it, the Debug section SHALL sit below the news, and the game-over screen's "Back to the map" SHALL be a plain button beside the primary New game. | replaced by FR-564 in part (the colour swatch beside each name is the nation's flag mark, `NationFlag`); replaced by FR-470 and FR-474 in part (M12b: the score is in the top bar, the table in the right pane's Nation group, closed by default; no Debug section below the news and no action buttons below it); tested; layout line not covered |
| FR-107 | News per nation: WHEN the news panel shows a resolved turn THEN each line from an event of another nation (its `nationId` is not the player's) SHALL start with that nation's name and a colon ("Valdoria: Rail finished at 15-8."). The player's own lines SHALL read as before, without a prefix, and come first; the other nations' lines follow; lines without a nation (game over) read as before and come last; each group keeps the event order. | tested; a Council meeting's line comes before the player's own lines (FR-306, M10) |
| FR-108 | No orders for another nation: the civilians of a nation other than the player's SHALL be drawn as markers, not buttons, and the store's `selectWorker` and `setSelectedWorkerId` SHALL ignore a worker that is not the player's. A click on another nation's tile or capital SHALL only select it for viewing: the HUD's tile panel shows the owner's name, the terrain and the resource and level by their display names (a resource not yet prospected is not named, as in FR-095) and the civilians with their jobs, and no control that builds an order (no job, construction or cancel button). Every order the UI builds SHALL carry the player's nation id (`activeNationId`). | tested |
| FR-109 | New game: the HUD SHALL have a New game button that asks in place before it acts ("Start a new game? This game will be lost.", buttons "Start new game" and "Keep playing"); the game-over screen SHALL have a New game button that acts at once. Both call the store's `startNewGame`, which (besides FR-105 (d)) SHALL clear the tile and worker selection and close every dialog (capital, market, warehouse, transport, construction), and the map is centred on the new capital (FR-083). | tested |
| FR-110 | Look fixes carried from M3.5: (a) every worker button SHALL be at least 24 x 24 px (WCAG 2.2 target size): a tile with one civilian of the player shows it as a 24 px button; a tile with several shows one 24 px button with their count (`worker-stack`), and while that tile is selected and no worker is, its civilians show as 24 px buttons in a tray beside the tile; (b) the selection ring SHALL be its own element drawn above the territory borders and rail lines (a z-index above the overlay), so no border or rail covers it; (c) `--color-nation-1` SHALL differ from `--color-bad` by a CIE Lab distance (ΔE76) of at least 25, the two nation colours from each other by at least 25, and also by at least 20 under simulated deuteranopia and protanopia; (d) `--color-text-danger` SHALL reach 4.5:1 on `--color-surface`, `--color-surface-sunken`, `--color-surface-raised` and `--color-bg-danger` (FR-094 test); (e) the palette scan (FR-086) SHALL also cover `fill`, `stroke`, `boxShadow` and the other colour properties, every CSS named colour, `hwb()`, `lab()`, `lch()`, `oklab()`, `oklch()`, `color()`, Tailwind colour classes and `layout.tsx`, with a self-check that it flags a sample of each; the M4 review added named colours in any case (`Red`, `RED`), `filter` and `backdrop-filter`, `background-image` gradients, logical borders (`border-inline-*`, `border-block-*`), `scrollbar-color`, `column-rule`, `-webkit-text-fill-color`, Tailwind arbitrary values (`text-[red]`), and in `.css` files outside `tokens.css` any named colour, hex or colour function on any line (comments excepted), so a value on the line after its property is found; (f) the M3 UI tests `scoreAndGameOver.test.tsx` and `warehouseAndHud.test.tsx` SHALL print no `act()` warning. | tested |
| FR-111 | Keyboard and screen reader use of the worker tray and New game (M4 review): (a) picking a worker from the tray SHALL return the focus to the stack button; (b) Escape SHALL close the tray (the tile stays selected) with the focus on the stack button, and the stack button opens it again; (c) the stack button's accessible name SHALL include the selected worker's type when one of its civilians is selected ("7 civilians, engineer selected"), else "7 civilians: choose one"; (d) every worker button SHALL expose `aria-pressed` (true when it is the selected worker); (e) the HUD New game toggle SHALL expose `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`, and opening it SHALL move the focus to the confirm panel (the panel itself, not Start new game, so a stray Enter does not end the game). | tested |

## M4.1 Play-test fixes

Written 2026-11-10 from Nathan's play-testing of M4 (NR-019 to NR-022). Branch `claude/m4-1-feedback`.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-112 | Civilians share tiles (NR-019): any number of civilians SHALL be able to stand on one land tile. A move (`moveWorker`, `moveAndBuildRail`, `moveAndStart*`) onto a tile where other civilians stand SHALL be validated exactly as onto an empty tile; no order is refused because of the civilians on a tile. | tested |
| FR-113 | One job per tile (NR-019; replaces the per-kind `tile-occupied` checks of FR-004 and FR-008): WHEN a tile already has a job (prospecting, development or construction, by any civilian) THEN every order that would start a second job there (`startProspecting`, `startDevelopment`, `startConstruction`, the `moveAndStart*` orders, `moveAndBuildRail`) SHALL be refused with the new reason `tile-has-job` ("another civilian is already working that tile"); `tile-occupied` is gone. The map's job cursor (the worker action helpers) SHALL not offer a job on such a tile. | tested |
| FR-114 | Where civilians appear (NR-019): a new game SHALL put each nation's start civilians on its capital tile (rules 03 section 2: a new civilian appears in the capital province), in a fixed order, so the capital holds a stack of seven. In v1 no other civilian is ever created: migrants (FR-042) and trainees (FR-041) join the industry workforce, not the map (the University is not built). | tested |
| FR-115 | Stacks on any tile in the UI (NR-019): the stack button with its count, the tray, the selection and the tile panel's civilian list SHALL work for several civilians on any tile, not only the capital. WHEN one of the player's civilians is selected and it stands on another tile THEN a click on this tile's civilian button or stack SHALL act as a click on the tile (the selected civilian's move or job order for this tile, FR-023) instead of selecting the civilian there; a click on the selected civilian's own button SHALL deselect it (`aria-pressed` back to false). | tested |
| FR-116 | Production plan (NR-020): one pure function `planProduction(nation)` SHALL compute what the production phase makes: the freight cars ordered first, then each building in production order makes min(order, capacity left, what the stock allows, what the labour left allows) (FR-037, unchanged). The production phase SHALL use it, so the plan and the phase never differ. A standing order whose inputs run short SHALL make as much as the inputs allow and stay as the standing order (rules 05: orders are saved between turns). | tested |
| FR-117 | Labour committed (NR-020; replaces FR-035's "standing production orders commit labour"): `labourOf(nation).committed` SHALL be the labour the production plan uses (the freight cars plus what the standing orders will really make), so an order that cannot run for lack of inputs commits no labour and blocks no other order. Total and free are as in FR-035. | tested |
| FR-118 | Raising an order (NR-020; replaces FR-036's stock and labour clauses): a `setProduction` above the current order SHALL be accepted only if the production plan with the new amount makes all of it and makes no less of any other output; otherwise it is refused with `insufficient-resources` when the stock (its own or shared with another order) is the limit, else `insufficient-labour`. Lowering an order stays always accepted; `invalid-target`, `invalid-amount` and `limit-reached` are as in FR-036. | tested |
| FR-119 | Capital dialog shows the plan (NR-020; extends FR-046): each output SHALL show "P of M", P the amount the production plan makes next production phase and M the most the engine accepts as a fresh order now (never below P); WHEN the standing order is above P THEN a note says "Order N: only P can be made (short of inputs)" or "(short of labour)". "+" sends P + batch and is disabled when that is above M; "−" sends max(0, P − batch) and is enabled while the order is above 0, so a stale order is cleared in one press. Removing an order and adding it back SHALL be accepted whenever the inputs and labour exist. | tested |
| FR-120 | Regression of the play-test bug (NR-020): from the real `newGame()`, with lumber 2, steel 2 and fabric 2 ordered on turn 1 and 6 turns resolved with no other order, on turn 7 (no timber, coal or iron left) the dialog SHALL show lumber and steel as 0 of 0 with the "short of inputs" note (not "2 of 2"), labour committed SHALL be fabric's 4, canned food SHALL be orderable, and removing fabric then adding it back SHALL be accepted. | tested |
| FR-121 | Commodity icons (NR-021): `COMMODITY_ICONS` SHALL give every warehouse commodity (resources, materials, goods) a Game Icon in the minimalist set (resources reuse `RESOURCE_ICONS`; new: lumber, paper, steel, fuel, fabric, canned food, clothing, furniture, hardware, armaments). The market dialog's commodity names and the capital dialog's output names and input lines SHALL show the icon before the name, decorative (`aria-hidden`); the name stays text. The palette scan (FR-086, FR-110 (e)) still passes. | replaced by FR-331 (M11: the pack's goods pictures before every commodity name; `COMMODITY_ICONS` removed, FR-335) |
| FR-122 | Purposeful rail and depots (NR-022; replaces the M4 engineer, which tried rail on every own tile in map order): the scripted engineer SHALL, in this order: (1) if a built depot of its nation is not joined to the capital's rail component, build the next rail tile of the shortest path (breadth-first over its own land where rail is allowed and no other job is under way, neighbours in map order) between that depot and the component; (2) else, if no depot is under construction, start a depot on the valid depot site (FR-030) that newly covers the most resource yield (the tile and its neighbours not yet on or next to the capital or a depot), at least 2 units, ties to the shorter rail path to the capital and then map order. It never lays rail that joins nothing. No port is built: the engine ships and trades without one. | replaced by FR-138 in part (clause (1)'s "next rail tile"; the rest holds); tested; clause (1) plans rail sections since FR-138; clause (2)'s choice of site replaced in part by FR-456 (the gain counts only what the nation will use, an urgent meal first) and FR-457 (the shortest payback first) in M12 strategy C |

expand one capacity point (FR-038), so they are worth 10 points as capacity and about 6 when sold.

capital"; nothing says that neighbouring rail tiles are joined by themselves.

A rail job records the tile its section starts from (`constructionJob.railFrom`); when the job finishes, its tile joins the graph linked to that one tile (a job without `railFrom`, from a hand-made test state, links to its first neighbouring node in map neighbour order). A finished depot or port joins the graph with no link: a section built into it or out of it joins it. The map's lines (FR-079), the depot and port lights (`transportConnectivitySystem`), logistics and the scripted player all read this one graph, so a depot is green only when drawn lines join it to the capital (NR-010). The start network (`initializeRailroadNetworks`) is unchanged: a map stores no sections, so rail placed on a map before play (hand-made test maps only) is linked to every neighbouring node; `newGame()` places no rail, so each nation starts with its capital alone, no link, as before. | tested |
| FR-137 | Which section an order builds (NR-026; replaces the rail-site clauses of FR-008 and FR-030): `moveAndBuildRail` onto a tile without rail (`canBuildRailAt`, unchanged) SHALL build the section from the engineer's tile when that is a rail node of the nation (rail, its capital, a depot or a port), else from the first neighbour of the target, in map neighbour order, that is one. `startConstruction` and `moveAndStartConstruction` with structure `rail` SHALL build the section from `fromTileId` when it is given (a rail node of the nation next to the tile; the tile may already have rail, so a section between two rail tiles closes a loop or joins two networks), and without it like `moveAndBuildRail` from a tile that is not a rail node. An order whose section is already built, or that finds no tile to start from, is refused with `invalid-site`; `fromTileId` on another structure with `invalid-target`. Terrain, owner, job and cost rules are unchanged (FR-029, FR-030, FR-113). The map's click policy (FR-023): with an engineer selected, a click on a neighbouring tile without rail tries rail-on-move; a click from a rail node on a neighbouring rail node of the nation not yet linked to it tries `moveAndStartConstruction` with `fromTileId` = the engineer's tile (the section between the two, so a depot whose rail meets the line head on can be joined); a click on a rail tile already linked to the engineer's tile, or not next to it, is a move. Each falls back to the plain move, except after `insufficient-funds` (FR-023). | replaced by FR-461 in part (the map's click policy: rail-on-move is the first order of an engineer's click on a neighbouring tile, the capital included, only where the engine's `railOnMoveStart` finds a section into it; where it finds none (the rail already reaches the tile, or the terrain refuses rail) the click is a plain move unless the head-on clause applies; the rest holds); tested; the head-on clause added in the M4.3 review fix round (regression test in tests/ui/tileClickOrders.test.ts) |
| FR-138 | Scripted rail by sections (NR-026; replaces FR-122 (1)'s "next rail tile" and FR-132's "rail tiles"): for a built depot not in the capital's rail component, the engineer SHALL take the shortest path (FR-122, unchanged) and its missing sections (consecutive path tiles not linked), and order the first missing section from the capital's end, else the first from the depot's end, as `moveAndStartConstruction` with `structure: "rail"` and `fromTileId`. `bestDepotSite`'s `railNeeded` and `railCost` (FR-132) SHALL count the missing sections, the one into the depot included, each priced by the terrain of the tile it is built on (the one farther from the capital). | tested |
| FR-139 | Rail regression (NR-026), from the real `newGame()` through `resolveTurn`: (a) the play-test case: rail on A next to the capital, then B next to A, then C next to both A and B, each built by the engineer standing on A, SHALL leave the links capital-A, A-B and A-C and no B-C, and the map SHALL draw exactly those; (b) a straight line of n sections SHALL be n links, drawn as one line; (c) a section built deliberately between B and C (both already rail) SHALL be a link and be drawn; (d) a depot SHALL be green exactly when the drawn lines join it to the capital: a depot finished next to rail is red, with no line to it, until a section is built into it. The scripted player's acceptance (FR-124, FR-125) still passes. | tested |

## M4.4 Recruiting AI (not built: only the furniture fix) and empty groups

What this milestone built: the furniture-limit fix (FR-140) and the transport popup's shipped-only and empty-group
rules (FR-141, FR-142). Recruiting workers for the computer nation was not built (NR-028; card Q21 is still open and
its option "no recruiting" is in force); FR-143, the row that measured recruiting first, was dropped.

Written 2026-11-12 from NR-027 and NR-028 (answers to decisions Q19 and Q20). Branch
`claude/m4-4-recruiting-ai`. Rules 04 section 5: resources, the materials steel, lumber and fabric and the
goods clothing, furniture and hardware can be shipped; paper, canned food, fuel and armaments never (made
only in the capital or bought). Rules 05 section 4: a migrant costs canned food + clothing + furniture,
at most floor(provinces / 4) a turn (FR-042: at least 1; the remake has no provinces, so 1), joins next
turn and eats from then on; section 5: each worker eats 1 raw food a turn (grain, fruit, grain,
livestock-or-fish by slot), canned food if the preferred food is missing, any other raw food makes him sick.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-140 | Furniture from unpaired lumber (NR-028; the bound FR-129 describes, "furniture from unpaired lumber"): the scripted player's run SHALL make at most floor((lumber − steel) / 2) furniture, counted from the stock at the start of the run (a furniture takes 2 lumber), so lumber that has a steel to pair with is never spent on furniture. With 4 lumber and 3 steel it makes none (before: 1, leaving 2 lumber with 3 steel); with 5 lumber and 1 steel, 2. | tested (tests/engine/scriptedScore.test.ts) |
| FR-141 | Only what can be shipped (NR-027): the transport popup SHALL list only the commodities rules 04 section 5 lets the network carry: every resource, the materials steel, lumber and fabric, and the goods clothing, furniture and hardware; never paper, canned food, fuel or armaments (`NEVER_SHIPPED` in `components/commodityKinds.ts`). A group keeps its FR-134 order without them. | tested (tests/ui/transportPopup.test.tsx) |
| FR-142 | Empty groups hidden (NR-027): the transport popup SHALL show a group only while at least one commodity it lists (FR-141) is not 0 / 0 for the active nation, that is, its tiles deliver some of it this turn (the slider's maximum) or some is allocated; the group then shows all the commodities it lists, and the shown groups keep the FR-134 order and the FR-135 columns, with no more columns than groups shown (two groups share the dialog's width; no empty third column). A group appears again as soon as one of its commodities has something to ship or is allocated. With no group to show, the popup says "Nothing to ship this turn." A new game (seed 1, nation 1: grain 4, fruit 4, cotton 2) therefore shows Food and Raw materials only, in two columns; in v1 nothing makes materials or goods to ship (no town production), so Materials and Goods stay hidden. On a 1280x720 screen the list still shows without scrolling. | tested (tests/ui/transportPopup.test.tsx, e2e/m4-3.spec.ts) |

## M6 Diplomacy

Written 2026-11-12 from `docs/REVIVAL_PLAN.md` section 5b M6 and rules 07 sections 1, 2, 5 and 7 (Imp1 manual
p75-85; Let's Play screens [07:W19] to [07:W22]) and 11 section 3 (Diplomacy screens). The step sizes of the
relation scale are hidden in Imp1 (gaps C1): the remake model is FR-172 (Q24). Not in v2 (Q37): Join the
Empire, intervention offers to pact partners, colony boycotts, the Council.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-161 | Overture orders (decision 5; Imp1 manual p77: diplomatic orders can be taken back until End Turn): five new order kinds SHALL exist: `proposeOverture { overture, targetId }`, overture "consulate", "embassy", "pact", "alliance", "war" or "peace"; `giveGrant { targetId, amount, locked }`; `setTradePolicy { targetId, subsidyPercent, boycott }`; `withdrawOverture { overture, targetId }` (an overture above, "grant" or "trade-policy"); `answerProposal { proposalId, accept }`. Applying one stores an intent (FR-152) and changes nothing of the target. Costs are taken from the treasury when ordered (cash, not credit; Imp1: "we do not have the $5000 required"): consulate $500, embassy $5,000 (Imp1 manual p79), a grant its amount. `withdrawOverture` removes this turn's intent and refunds its cost in full (Imp1 manual p77); "grant" with no grant ordered this turn ends a standing locked grant from End Turn, no refund; any other withdrawal with nothing to take back changes nothing, also when applied without validation (review F2). A second `giveGrant` or `setTradePolicy` for the same target in a turn replaces the first (refund, then charge). Each kind is in `ORDER_KINDS`, `validateOrder`, `applyValidOrder` and `ORDER_TEXT`. Tests: tests/engine/orders/diplomacyOrders.test.ts, tests/engine/validate.test.ts, tests/engine/apply.test.ts. | tested (tests/engine/orders/diplomacyOrders.test.ts, tests/engine/validate.test.ts, tests/engine/apply.test.ts; ORDER_TEXT in tests/ui/gameText.test.ts) |
| FR-162 | Overture refusals: `validateOrder` SHALL refuse with these reasons (five new ones, texts in `REFUSAL_TEXT`: `needs-consulate` "we need a consulate there first", `needs-embassy` "we need an embassy there first", `already-in-place` "that is already in place", `at-war` "we are at war with them", `not-at-war` "we are not at war with them"): an unknown target or the nation itself → `invalid-target`; a consulate already held or already ordered this turn → `already-in-place`; an embassy, a pact or an alliance already held or already ordered this turn, or peace already proposed this turn → `already-in-place` (review A-R7); an embassy without a consulate established before this turn → `needs-consulate`; a pact or a grant without an embassy → `needs-embassy`; a pact with a Great Power or an alliance with a minor → `invalid-target`; a consulate, embassy, pact, alliance, grant or subsidy while at war with the target → `at-war`; war while at war or already declared this turn → `already-in-place`; peace while not at war → `not-at-war`; a grant other than 1,000, 3,000, 5,000 or 10,000, or a subsidy other than 0, 5, 10, 25, 50, 75 or 100 → `invalid-amount`; a subsidy above 0 without a consulate established before this turn → `needs-consulate` (Imp1 wiki: not on the consulate's own turn, [07:W2]); a cost above the treasury → `insufficient-funds`; an `answerProposal` for a proposal not addressed to the nation or expired → `invalid-target`; a `withdrawOverture` with nothing to withdraw → `invalid-target`. One row per refusal in tests/engine/validate.test.ts; texts in tests/ui/gameText.test.ts. | tested (tests/engine/validate.test.ts; texts in tests/ui/gameText.test.ts) |

It allows a subsidy from the next turn and is needed for an embassy. Event `overture-done`. Example: turn 1 Testland orders a consulate with Arvelia: treasury 10,000 → 9,500 at once; after End Turn `consulates` of "minor-1\|nation-1" holds nation-1. Accepted: `alliance` is set from that End Turn; accepted in a turn in which a war between the two is declared, it fails instead (`overture-failed`, reason `at-war`, for the proposer; review A-R5). Both Great Powers proposing an alliance to each other in the same turn agree at once, whatever FR-174 says, and no proposal is made (review A-R6). Declaring war on an ally ends the alliance (FR-168). Events `overture-done`, `overture-refused`, and `proposal-received` for the human (its `nationId` is the receiving human, `fromId` the proposer, FR-176). | FR-236 | Dialog behaviour (QA item 1, the WAI-ARIA dialog pattern; closes the dialog debt of M4.3 review finding 7): every game dialog (Diplomacy, Accept Offer, Warehouse, Capital, Market, Transportation of Commodities, Construction Options, About, Game over) SHALL be built on one shared component, `components/Modal.tsx`, and SHALL: be `role="dialog"` with `aria-modal="true"` and `aria-labelledby` its visible title, so its name is the title ("Diplomacy — Testland", "World market — Testland", "About"); WHEN it opens THEN move the focus into it, to its first control (Accept Offer: the dialog itself, so Enter still accepts, FR-175; WHEN the next offer shows in the same dialog THEN the focus moves to the dialog again, whatever button was clicked, QA review F2); WHEN it closes THEN give the focus back to the element that had it when it opened (the button that opened it) while that element is still on the page; keep Tab and Shift+Tab inside it, from the last control to the first and back; from a focused spot that is not a control (the dialog itself, the war question's group, a disabled control) Tab SHALL go to the next control after it and Shift+Tab to the one before it (QA review F1); WHEN an order disables or removes the focused control and the browser moves the focus to the page THEN Tab and Shift+Tab SHALL go on from that control's place (the control after or before it, or the one now in its place), not from the top of the dialog (QA review F4); WHEN Escape is pressed THEN close it (Accept Offer: Escape rejects, FR-175; while the Diplomacy screen's war question is open, Escape answers it "Keep the peace" first, wherever the focus is in the dialog, and the focus goes back to "Declare war", FR-178, QA review F3); WHEN a click starts and ends on the backdrop outside the dialog THEN close it (Accept Offer: as its × button, FR-175). Only the top dialog of a stack (Diplomacy over Capital, the transport plan over Capital) answers the keys and its backdrop. The Game over dialog has no close: Escape and its backdrop do nothing (its "Back to the map" and "New game" buttons stay). The About dialog opens over the whole page, as the others do (it was a panel inside the HUD).
