Written 2026-11-12 from `docs/REVIVAL_PLAN.md` section 5b M7, rules 06 sections 2 to 5 and 7 (Imp1 manual
p66-74; Imp1 Let's Play boards, offer sheets, Trade Book and ledgers [06:W30] to [06:W38]) and 07 sections 2
and 3. Minor offers and demand, the favourite-partner weights and the clearing price are hidden or unmeasured in
Imp1 (gaps A2, A3, C5): remake defaults, decision cards Q26 to Q28.

|---|---|---|
| FR-182 | Market mode (decision 1): `state.market.mode` SHALL be "world" (the v1 world market, FR-060 to FR-065 and FR-103, unchanged) or "partners" (FR-183 to FR-192); two-islands builds "world", continent "partners", and `newGame({ market })` overrides it (FR-158 runs the continent with "world"). In "partners" nothing is bought from or sold to the world: every deal has a counterparty. | FR-186 | Matching (Imp1 manual p66, p70-72; Imp1 Board of Trade order [06:W30]): the trade phase SHALL settle the commodities in the Imp1 Board order (clothing, furniture, hardware, armaments, canned food, fabric, lumber, paper, steel, cotton, wool, timber, coal, iron ore, horses, then any other tradeable commodity in type order), each in three steps: (1) each minor's offer, minors in id order, goes unit by unit to the Great Powers that bid for it and may trade with that minor, in the minor's preference order (FR-187), while the bid is not filled, the buyer has a free hold (FR-188) and can pay (FR-189); (2) each minor's demand, minors in id order, is filled from the Great Powers that offer the commodity and may trade with that minor, in the minor's preference order, each unit taking a hold of the seller; (3) what is left of a Great Power's offer goes to what is left of the other Great Power's bid, each unit taking a hold of the buyer. A unit a country cannot take passes to the next in order. Examples: Arvelia offers 3 coal, Testland (favourite) bids 2 and Valdoria 4 → Testland 2, Valdoria 1; Valdoria offers 2 steel, Testland bids 3 steel and no minor sells steel → Testland buys Valdoria's 2 with 2 of its own holds. A bid keeps reserving amount x P when ordered (FR-062); at settlement the deal price comes out of that reserve, any extra from the treasury (a unit the treasury cannot cover is not bought: `offer-missed` with "no-cash"), and the unused reserve is refunded; offered units leave the warehouse when ordered (FR-061) and the unsold ones return at the end of the trade phase; bought units arrive next turn (FR-062). ## M8 Armies

Written 2026-11-12 from `docs/REVIVAL_PLAN.md` section 5b M8 and rules 08 sections 1, 2, 5 and 7 (Imp1 manual
p36-41, p60, p91; Imp1 Armoury and Garrison Book screens [08:W20], [08:W25]; Imp1 ledgers for upkeep) and 11
sections 4 and 5. Rail moves, landings, generals and medals are not in v2 (Q37).

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-199 | Era-1 regiments (stats Imp1 manual p91; costs rules 08 sections 1 and 7.1): `definisions/regiments.ts` SHALL hold, for the 9 era-1 types with their Imp1 display names, FPN / FPM / RNG / DEF (entrenched) / MVR, ARMS, worker grade, horses and cash: Minutemen 5/5/5/4(5)/4, militia, no cost; Skirmishers 5/5/5/7(8)/6, 1, untrained, $200 (Imp1 screen); Regulars 10/10/5/5(6)/4, 1, untrained, $500 (Imp1 wiki [?]); Grenadiers 12/12/5/5(6)/4, 1, trained, $1,000 (Imp1 screen, by subtraction [?]); Hussars 7/10/3/7/11, 1, untrained, 1 horse, $100 (derived from the 1998 guide, low [?]); Cuirassiers 15/19/3/5/9, 1, trained, 1 horse, $500 (derived, medium); Light Artillery 10/3/9(10)/3(4)/5, 2, trained, 1 horse, $1,000 (derived, low [?]); Artillery 16/4/11(12)/2(3)/3, 2, trained, no horse, $1,000 (Imp1 screen); Sappers 0/0/5/3(4)/4, 2, expert, $5,000 (derived, medium). A type entrenches only if it has an entrenched DEF and is not artillery (Imp1 manual p37-38, v1.0 readme): Minutemen, Skirmishers, Regulars, Grenadiers, Sappers. `types/Unit.ts` (`UnitStats`, `RegimentCategory`) is used. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] Q31: the derived costs stand until a screen shows them. Tests: tests/engine/regiments.test.ts. | tested (tests/engine/regiments.test.ts) |
| FR-200 | Recruiting (plan M8; Imp1 Armoury: cash and stock fall at once, the left arrow cancels, regiments appear the turn after End Turn [08:W20]): a new order `setRecruitment { regiment, count }` SHALL set how many of that regiment the nation recruits this turn, like `setProduction`: raising it takes the difference's cash, armaments, horses and workers (one worker of the regiment's grade each, out of the industry workforce) at once, lowering it gives them back. Refused: a world without provinces (two-islands), a militia or a Sapper type, or a nation that does not hold its capital province (the recruits join there, FR-201) → `invalid-target` (Sappers dig walls, which v2 does not model, Q31); a count that is not a whole number from 0 → `invalid-amount`; cash → `insufficient-funds`; armaments or horses → `insufficient-resources`; no worker of the grade whose labour the production plan (FR-116) leaves free → `insufficient-labour`. Example: $10,000, 2 armaments, 4 untrained workers: 2 Regulars → $9,000, 0 armaments, 2 untrained; a third → `insufficient-resources`. Tests: tests/engine/armies.test.ts, tests/engine/validate.test.ts. | tested (tests/engine/armies.test.ts, tests/engine/validate.test.ts); the capital-province refusal added in the M8 review fix round (R4, E4) |

WHEN the nation no longer holds its capital province at that step (M9: taken in this End Turn's combat) THEN none is raised: the cash, armaments, horses and workers paid for them come back, with one `recruits-refunded { nationId, regiment, count }` per type. WHEN the treasury cannot pay THEN the step SHALL disband the newest regiments (highest id) one at a time until it can, then pay, with `regiments-disbanded { nationId, reason: "no-pay" }`. control in v2.

## M10 Council of Governors

Written 2026-11-13 from `docs/REVIVAL_PLAN.md` section 5b M10 and NR-011 (decision Q12: the game ends as the original
does, by a Council vote, once one nation is strong enough; the 40-turn end stays as the fallback), rules 01 sections 4
and 4a (Imp1 manual p14, p19: one governor and one vote per province, a winner needs more than two thirds of all votes,
only the two leading powers are nominated, a nominee gets every province it owns, colonies included, Minor Nation votes
follow relations, many governors abstain early, at the last meeting every governor must choose; Let's Play Council
screens [01:W20] with the four rows Controlled, Colonies, Other Great Powers, Other Minor Nations; Other Great Powers'
provinces went to the militarily strongest nominee [01:W20]) and rules 11 section 8.1 (the vote screen: title "Council
of Governors", the two nominees with crest, total and four rows, "<Nation> Wins!" on the winning screen, no controls,
the player does not vote). Imp1 has 120 votes and needs 81; the remake's continent has 12 provinces, so 12 votes and 9
needed. Imp1 meets every 10 years over 100 years (gaps E1: the nomination weights and the governors' rule are hidden);
the remake meets every 10 turns over its 40, with the simple rules below. Every number marked [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] is a
remake default and a decision card (Q50 to Q56). Map ballot icons and Council Projection charts are not in M10.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-290 | Council meetings (Imp1 manual p19: about every 10 years, 1825 first; remake scale): `definisions/council.ts` SHALL hold `COUNCIL = { firstTurn: 10, interval: 10, minRelationPoints: 50, provinceWeight: 100, militaryWeight: 10 }`. WHEN a turn numbered 10, 20, 30 or 40 (`firstTurn + k x interval`, at most `turnLimitOf(state)`) resolves on a world with provinces and that turn did not end the game by conquest (FR-223) THEN the Council SHALL meet once, on the state after the clock advances (so provinces taken in that turn's combat count). No meeting on a world without provinces (two-islands), on a turn not in the list, or on a game with `turnLimit` below 10. `nextCouncilTurn(state)` SHALL return the next meeting turn at or after `state.turn` (10 on a new continent game, 20 on turn 11, 40 on turn 40), undefined on two-islands, after turn 40 and after game over. Dates: turn 10 Summer 1817, 20 Winter 1819, 30 Summer 1822, 40 Winter 1824. [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] Q50. Tests: tests/engine/council.test.ts. | tested (tests/engine/council.test.ts); `isCouncilTurn(state, turn)` and `councilMeeting(state, turn)` (what `resolveTurn` calls, on the state after the clock); with a turn limit above 40 the meetings go on every 10 turns (50, 60, ...); the dates come from `yearOfTurn` (`engine/calendar.ts`) |
| FR-291 | Standing and nominees (Imp1: only the two leading powers are nominated, from interior, diplomatic and military strength, "size counts" [01:W22]; weights hidden, gaps E1; formula remake): `councilStanding(state, nationId)` SHALL be `scoreOf(state, nationId).total` (FR-067) + 100 x the provinces the Great Power holds + floor(10 x `militaryStrength(state, nationId)`) (FR-174). Example: score 650, 5 provinces, strength 36 → 650 + 500 + 360 = 1,510. The nominees SHALL be the two Great Powers holding at least one province with the highest standing, highest first (ties: `state.nations` order); with fewer than two such Great Powers no meeting is held. ## M11 Ink-and-wash restyle (NR-032)

Written 2026-11-14 from NR-032 (Nathan's answer "After M9"), NR-031's rules, `docs/art/ink-and-wash-INVENTORY.md`,
`docs/art/ink-and-wash-HANDOFF.md`, the pack's `manifest.json` and demo, and three code maps of the map, the icons and
the plumbing (2026-11-14). Facts checked in the code at spec time: tiles 42 px with odd rows shifted 21 px and a
1,029 x 672 px map (`styles/theme.ts` TILE_PX, `camera.ts` mapSizePx, `MapView.module.css` .rowOdd), the same formulas
as the pack's demo; 19 `TerrainType`, 13 `ResourceType`, 6 `MaterialType`, 4 `GoodsType`, 8 civilian `WorkerType`
(`types/Workers.ts`), 16 `IndustryType`; the pack's 23 goods, 8 workers, 4 worker icons and 16 buildings match them one
to one; the real worlds use only water, farm, orchard, openRange, plantation, hardwoodForest, barrenHills and capital
(`engine/world/twoIslands.ts`, `continent.ts`), so the other terrains are tested on hand-built tiles.

M11 draws only. No game rule changes and no file under `src/app/engine`, `src/app/store` or `src/app/types` changes.
Every element keeps its text label, accessible name, title, test id and data attributes: the art never replaces a
name, and a picture beside a name is decorative (`alt=""`). A pack picture carries `data-art` = its manifest `file`
(`data-art="goods/steel.png"`), as `data-icon` names a Game Icon today. Where the pack has no file for an element, the
row says what stays (today's Game Icon) and a decision card records it (Q60 to Q76, below the table). When an M11 row
turns tested, the older row it replaces gets "replaced by FR-###": FR-084, FR-085 (the tile icon), FR-086 (the
per-terrain tint), FR-087 (the ring's colour), FR-090, FR-093 (the dots' drawing), FR-121, FR-133, FR-154 (its 3:1
clause).

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-318 | The one table, complete (NR-032; extends FR-272): `components/art/inkArt.ts` SHALL map, by game id: (a) every `TerrainType` to its map picture or to none: dryPlains `terrain/dry-plains.png`, openRange `open-range.png`, horseRanch `horse-ranch.png`, plantation `plantation.png`, farm `farm.png`, orchard `orchard.png`, fertileHills `wool-pasture.png` when the tile holds wool and `fertile-hills.png` without a resource (Q62), barrenHills `barren-hills.png`, mountains `mountains.png`, hardwoodForest `hardwood-forest.png`, scrubForest `scrub-forest.png`, swamp `swamp.png`, desert `desert.png`, tundra `tundra.png`, town `town.png`, capital `capital.png`; water, river and coast none; (b) `improvementArt(resourceType, level)`: for levels 1 to 3 grain `improvements/farm-N.png`, fruit `orchard-N`, cotton `plantation-N`, livestock `ranch-N`, wool `wool-N`, timber `forestry-N`, coal, ironOre, gold and gems `mine-N`, oil `oil-well-N`; none for fish and horses (no tiers; `horse-ranch-1.png` is a variant, not a level) and none at level 0 (the terrain picture shows); (c) every warehouse commodity (13 resources, 6 materials, 4 goods) to `goods/` (ironOre `iron-ore.png`, cannedFood `canned-food.png`, every other id the same name), never by reading the manifest's labels (its "Cast Iron / Steel" is the game's Steel); (d) every civilian `WorkerType` of `types/Workers.ts` to `workers/<same id>.png`; (e) the worker grades untrained, trained and expert (`WorkerGrade`, `types/Industry.ts`) and labour to `worker-icons/`; (f) every `IndustryType` to `buildings/` (refinery `oil-refinery.png`, armoury `armory.png`, the other camelCase ids in kebab-case); (g) the depot and port (`infrastructure/depot.png`, `port.png`), the rail piece `infrastructure/rail-straight.svg`, `map-ui/tile-selection.svg`, `map-ui/station-connected.svg` and `station-disconnected.svg`; (h) the ground textures `terrain/grass-base.png`, `open-water.png`, `desert-base.png`, `tundra-base.png`. ### M11 decision cards

Defaults taken by the spec; each is on the progress page for Nathan.

1. **Q60. Keep Game Icons where the pack has no picture?** The pack has nothing for the market scales, Deal Book, Diplomacy, Center, Advance Turn, final scores, About, New game, a group of civilians, the three job markers, the army chip's tent and pitchfork, attack, battle, Council and the river. Default: those keep their Game Icon (FR-335); `react-icons` and the About credit stay. Alternative: drop them to text only, or draw new art.
2. **Q61. Cursors from the pack's figures?** Default: 32 px cursors drawn at runtime from the prospector, the developing civilian, the fort, the rail piece and the engineer (FR-330); emoji removed; `pointer` until ready. Alternative: keep the emoji cursors (against NR-032), or plain system cursors.
3. **Q62. Which picture for fertile hills?** The pack has `wool-pasture.png` (sheep, no fence) and `fertile-hills.png` (bare hills). Default: wool pasture on a tile with wool, bare hills without a resource. No real world has fertile hills yet.
4. **Q63. How does a mine or oil well say which mineral?** Default: a 16 px goods mark (coal, iron ore, gold, gems, oil) at the tile's bottom-left once the resource is known (FR-323). Alternative: no mark (the tile info panel names it).
5. **Q64. Keep the "L1" to "L3" badge now that the picture shows the level?** Default: keep it (a text cue, pinned by tests). Alternative: drop it.
6. **Q65. How big are depot and port on a tile?** Default (QA fix round, FR-347; review F1, FR-356): 20 px pictures in the tile's top-right corner, each box cropped to the drawing's 14 rows, with the pack's station mark at 11 px on its top-right; a depot and a port on one tile side by side, the depot rightmost, the level badge and job markers then below them; civilians on a tile with a station in its corner, below the drawings. Stacked (the QA round's first try) the port lay under the civilians' button; the first default, 14 px with 8 px marks along the top edge (FR-324), was a speck at 1x and its marks could not be told apart. Alternatives: the depot or port as the tile's main picture (as the demo), losing the terrain picture there; or smaller stations while civilians stand there (no badge or job moves, but the speck returns).
7. **Q66. The pack's corner brackets for the selection?** Default: yes, in place of the teal inset ring (FR-326). Alternative: keep the teal ring.
8. **Q67. Borders: nation colours on a pale halo?** The pack's territory line is one brown, so it cannot show which nation owns the land. Default: the vector borders stay in nation colours on the pack's halo colour; minor and province colours are darkened to reach 3:1 on the halo (FR-327, FR-329). Alternative: the pack's brown line for all borders plus another ownership cue. The minor's colour (lane 1): the old slate darkened to 3:1 came within 20 of nation 1 under protanopia (FR-154), so the default is #26362c (dark slate green, 6.14:1 on the halo, colour-blind margin to nation 1 only 20.0); alternatives: the hue-kept near black #192127, or relax FR-154's colour-blind distance for the dashed minor line.
9. **Q68. Rail: the pack's straight piece turned along every link?** Default: yes, diagonals stretched from 42 to 47 px; the curve and bridge samples unused (FR-328). Alternative: keep the plain dark line.
10. **Q69. March arrows stay straight?** Default: yes (FR-273's decision kept, so FR-270 and FR-271 hold); the pack's curved green arrow stays unused.
11. **Q70. Ground: one shoreline drawing under the whole map?** Default: a port of the demo's shoreline with textures repeating at their own size, the sea past the map's edge in the ground's water colour (FR-321). Alternative: one texture per tile (cheaper, square coasts).
12. **Q71. Retire the per-terrain tile tints?** Default: tiles become transparent over the ground; the 19 `--terrain-*` tokens and `TERRAIN_TINT` are removed in M11's closing step with their palette test. Alternative: keep them as a fallback colour.
13. **Q72. Civilians at 20 px?** The pack's figures were reviewed at 42 px; the button must stay 24 px (FR-110). Default: 20 px figures in the 24 px button (FR-325). Alternative: larger buttons (more overlap on a crowded tile).
14. **Q73. Capital buildings on the existing cards, or a city view?** Default: pictures on the existing cards and panels (FR-333); no city view; shipyard and power plant unused. Alternative: a new city screen (a separate milestone).
15. **Q74. Draw forts on the map?** The game does not draw them today and every capital has a level-1 fort. Default: no fort mark on the map in M11.
16. **Q75. Add pictures to lists that have none today?** Default: yes for the Warehouse rows, the Labour panel and the tile info panel's resource and civilian lines (FR-331, FR-332); the terrain line stays text.
17. **Q76. List picture size?** The goods art was reviewed at 22 px; list text is 12 to 14 px. Default: 1.25em on the text line (about 17 px), within today's fits (FR-331). Alternative: 22 px with taller rows (the Board of Trade and Armoury fits would need changing).
18. **Q77. A crowded capital: how does it stay recognisable?** (review F2) The civilians' 24 px button covers the middle of any picture that fills the 40 px tile, and a capital holds the start civilians and the army chip, so on turn 1 only the spire showed. Default: on a capital or town with civilians or a minor's name tag the picture moves to the top centre at 24 px, above the button (FR-341), as the Game Icon did before M11. Alternatives: move the button and the army chip off the picture (they would then cover the neighbouring tiles), or keep the whole-tile picture covered and add a text cue. QA fix round (FR-346): the 24 px picture still showed only a small dome at 1x; the default is now the picture at 48 px, lifted 18 px into the gap between the row above's pictures, the civilians in the content box's corner and the army chip low (a minor's stays high, above its name tag): on turn 1 at most 22% of each capital's drawing is covered.
19. **Q78. Transport's picture at 16 px?** (QA fix round) The railyard at 16 px in the HUD and the Transport heading was a brown blob; the depot, the pack's other transport picture, is no clearer at that size (a grey roof and a smoke plume). Default: keep the railyard and draw it larger: 20 px in the HUD, every full-width action button's icon in the same 20 px box so the labels stay aligned (FR-349, FR-351), and 30 px in the Capital's Transport heading like the other building headings (FR-352). Alternatives: the depot; text only in the HUD.

a missed list of FR-354 (its status names it); F2 is a test that could not fail (tests/ui/accessibleNames.test.tsx now

## M12 Honest game (NR-033)

Written 2026-11-15 from NR-033, the audit of 2026-11-14 (`docs/audit/2026-11-14/*.json`: 8 tracks, 160 findings, each
with its repro, expected, actual and suspect line) and the orchestrator's plan (themes T1 to T10; T11 to T16 are M13 to
M18 in `docs/REVIVAL_PLAN.md`). Each row cites the finding ids it fixes; duplicate findings are one row. M12 fixes what a
player can see wrong or what lies to the player; it adds no game system (no technology, University, ships, credit or
recruiting computer).

Rules for every M12 row: every accessible name, title, test id and data attribute stays unless the row says otherwise (a
price or a reason goes into a visible line beside or under a control, never into its name); every new dialog renders
`components/Modal.tsx` (FR-236, pinned by tests/ui/dialogs.test.tsx); the 1280x720 layout pins (FR-088, FR-268, FR-315,
FR-346, FR-356) hold; the engine stays headless and pure, and saving lives in the store; no colour literal outside
`styles/tokens.css` and `styles/theme.ts`. "L1" to "L4" name the implementer lane of the M12 plan (L1 engine and store
honesty, L2 map and tile UI, L3 shell and dialogs, L4 docs). "Verify" names the evidence: a unit test (Vitest), an e2e
spec (Playwright) or a by-eye screenshot for the QA pass. A file marked (new) did not exist when the row was written (all exist now). Defaults the spec took
are cards Q79 to Q104 under the table.

Status of FR-357 to FR-443 (2026-11-16, M12 close-out): each row was set from the lane notes (`.gate/m12/L1-notes.md` to `L4-notes.md`,
`integration-notes.md`, `ai-margin-notes.md`) and from the tests whose titles name its id; every test file a status names exists, and the
unit test files of 78 of the 87 rows were run again, 36 files, one at a time, all passing (journal, evidence table). Result: 77 tested, 9 partial, 1 parked.
Partial: FR-365 and FR-388 (a clause no test checks, or a clause the code does not meet) and FR-435 to FR-441 (document rows, checked by a node
script or by reading; no test of the suite reads those documents). Parked: FR-372 (the 8-line news list; the HUD milestone M12b closes it).
The gate on the close-out tree: `npm test` `Tests  2446 passed (2446)`; Playwright `92 passed` and `1 skipped` (FR-372's `test.fixme`).

e2e/m12-qa.spec.ts red before the change). Names and test ids stay; no engine, store or type file changes.

### M12 close-out rows (2026-11-16, NR-034)

Four rows from the close-out of M12: three small follow-ups to the QA fix round and one guard for the v1 games. All four
were built and passed gate 5 on 2026-11-16; each status names its tests. Their wording follows the close-out
brief; the tests the code agent writes are the exact check, and a difference between a row and its test is a defect of the
row.

WHEN `railOnMoveStart` finds a section THEN rail-on-move is the first order, onto the capital too: an engineer on a plain tile next to a capital that the rail beside it does not yet join lays that section by the click, as FR-378's case (a click from the line's last rail node onto the capital, `moveOrders`) does. FR-446 made the plain move only for a step along laid rail. ### M12 decision cards

Defaults taken by the spec; each goes on the progress page (FR-437). Each card: the question, the default taken, the
alternative, and the strongest objection to the default.

Q79 to Q83 are the orchestrator's five defaults, already on the progress page under these numbers (roadmap.json,
2026-11-15); Q84 to Q104 are the spec's; Q105 is the strategy C gate's (FR-159, 2026-11-16); Q106 and Q107 are the second delta review's (FR-125, FR-460, 2026-11-16).

Answered by Nathan, 2026-11-16 (in chat: "Q79 your suggestion, Q80, Q81, Q82, Q83 your suggestion"): Q79 to Q83 are decided as
the defaults below, which M12 built (FR-357 to FR-363, FR-389 and FR-390, FR-371 and FR-373, FR-383, FR-386 and FR-422,
FR-406 and FR-407). Q102 is ruled the other way: Nathan said "keep the debug": the bug-report details
stay and show without `?debug`, in the HUD's closed group "Details for bug reports" (FR-476). So Q102's default below
and FR-377 no longer hold: M12b built FR-476 (2026-11-17, branch `claude/m12b-hud`). Q105 to Q107 are still open.

1. **Q79. Should the game save itself automatically, in this browser only?** (FR-357 to FR-363) Default: one autosave
   slot in `localStorage`, written after every End Turn and within a second of every accepted order, versioned (a save
   of another version is refused with a message); on start a "Saved game" dialog offers Continue (focused) and New game,
   and with no save the game starts at once; New game keeps its existing question; the leave-page warning appears only
   while a write is pending or failed. Alternatives: several slots; a save file to download. Objection: one slot means a
   bad late move cannot be undone by loading an earlier turn, and clearing the browser's site data deletes the game.
2. **Q80. Should a transport slider you set be a hard limit?** (FR-389, FR-390) Default: a slider the player set is a
   hard cap for that commodity; one never touched is "automatic" and shares the spare capacity as today; spare capacity
   never goes to a set commodity. Alternative: sliders as priorities only (capacity always used). Objection: a forgotten
   low slider can starve a factory without a message, and the scripted player sets every commodity, so it loses today's
   spare-capacity top-up and its scores move (FR-405).
3. **Q81. Where should refusals and errors be shown?** (FR-371, FR-373) Default: a status line at the bottom centre of
   the map for 6 seconds, and Tile Info at the top of the HUD under turn, cash and score. Alternative: the reason in the
   news box only. Objection: the line can cover tiles at the map's lower edge, and a tall Tile Info pushes the news down.
4. **Q82. Should a costly order ask once before it spends?** (FR-383, FR-386, FR-422) Default: any one order of $1,000
   or more (every fort is $5,000) from the map, Tile Info, Construction Options or a grant shows its price and asks once;
   the cancel text says what money comes back. Alternatives: show the price and never ask; ask for every order.
   Objection: one more click for an experienced player on every depot, port, fort and level 2 or 3 job.
5. **Q83. Should war on a minor with no land border be blocked until ships exist?** (FR-406, FR-407) Default: refused by
   the engine (`no-land-route`) and the button disabled with the reason until M17. Alternative: allow the declaration
   for its effect on relations. Objection: it removes the option to declare such a war only to change relations.
6. **Q84. Centre and zoom the map?** (FR-367, FR-368) Default: centre on an axis only when more than 64 px are spare (so
   1280x720 is unchanged), and zoom steps of 100%, 150% and 200%. Alternative: always centre; free zoom with the wheel.
   Objection: zoom touches every map layer (ground, rail, borders, arrows, cursors); if it cannot keep the 100% checks
   green inside the lane, the row parks as blocked rather than shipping half done.
7. **Q85. How does the keyboard reach the map?** (FR-369) Default: the map is one Tab stop; the arrow keys move between
   tiles, Enter acts. Alternative: every tile a Tab stop (384 stops). Objection: screen readers then hear only the
   focused tile, not the whole map.
8. **Q86. Keep the plain-move fallback of a refused job?** (FR-374) Default: keep FR-023's fallback (a refused job still
   moves the civilian) and say so in the status line, with Cancel to undo. Alternative: no fallback; a refused job moves
   nothing. Objection: a player who misses the line still loses the civilian's turn.
9. **Q87. Clicking another civilian: select it or move onto it?** (FR-385) Default: select it; a move onto that tile is a
   click on the tile beside the button. Alternative: keep "move here" and switch with a modifier key. Objection: moving
   onto a crowded tile needs a more careful click.
10. **Q88. Draw forts on the map?** (FR-388; replaces Q74's default "no") Default: a 16 px fort mark on every tile with a
    fort, "Fort level 1" in its title and in Tile Info. Alternative: Tile Info only. Objection: one more mark on the
    crowded capital tiles.
11. **Q89. Trade orders are forgotten every turn: what helps?** (FR-397 to FR-399) Nathan settled Q15 on 2026-11-09
    (NR-018): one-turn trade orders, a repeat button "if play feels tedious"; the audit found it tedious (MKT-04,
    RULES-GAP-12: up to four clicks a turn for 40 turns). Default: that repeat button on the Board of Trade, built from
    the order log, with no engine change, so no baseline moves. Alternative: standing orders as in Imp1 (rules 06
    section 2), an engine change that would replace NR-018. Objection: still one click a turn, and Imp1 kept orders by
    itself.
12. **Q90. How does a tie between Great Powers for a minor's favour break?** (FR-401) Default: an order fixed per minor
    for the whole game, derived from the seed, so the favourite never flips by turn parity and neither nation is
    favoured overall. Alternative: the rules' fixed Great Power order (always nation 1 first), against architecture
    decision 5. Objection: one nation may hold the tie with a given minor for the whole game by luck of the seed.
13. **Q91. Should the score stop punishing investment?** (FR-402, FR-403) Default: unfinished paid jobs count at their
    price / 100, and level 3 is worth 50 points (its $5,000). Alternative: keep FR-067 and only explain it (FR-404).
    Objection: it changes every score and the scripted player's numbers, and NR-018 had kept the score weights for v1.
14. **Q92. When does the computer ask for peace?** (FR-408) Default: only after it lost a province or a battle in this
    war, and not again for 6 turns after a refusal; its answers to the player's offers stay (FR-174). Alternative: keep
    "weaker than the enemy" and only slow the offers down. Objection: a computer that is badly outnumbered but not yet
    beaten never offers peace.
15. **Q93. What does closing Accept Offer do?** (FR-410) Default: it hides the dialog; the offer stays answerable in
    Diplomacy until End Turn, then is refused. Alternative: the × refuses at once, after asking. Objection: an offer can
    be forgotten in Diplomacy and refused by default.
16. **Q94. Council nominees: by standing or by votes?** (FR-413, FR-414) Default: the screen keeps FR-313's standing
    order and says so; the news line orders by votes. Alternative: order both by votes. Objection: the screen and the
    news then list the nominees in different orders (as card Q58 already notes).
17. **Q95. Guard Advance Turn how?** (FR-423, FR-432) Default: a 600 ms lock after each End Turn and a "civilians without
    orders" line, but no question. Alternative: ask "End the turn with 7 civilians idle?". Objection: an idle End Turn on
    purpose is common late in a game, so a question would nag; the line only informs.
18. **Q96. Are phones supported?** (FR-428, FR-429) Default: no; below 700 px a line says the game is made for 1024 px and
    wider, the map pans by touch, and the Capital, Board of Trade and Diplomacy dialogs stay readable and closable.
    Alternative: a full phone layout (the HUD as a bottom sheet). Objection: the page's viewport tag invites phones.
19. **Q97. Make the Capital dialog fit 1280x720, or show that it scrolls?** (FR-426) Default: an always-visible scrollbar
    and a fade on a column that does not fit. Alternative: a denser layout that fits 1280x720 (FR-352's fits are
    already tight). Objection: the Clothing factory card still starts below the fold.
20. **Q98. When does "How to play" open?** (FR-430, FR-431) Default: on the first new game in a browser, and from About at
    any time. Alternative: on every new game; or a tutorial that walks through turn 1. Objection: a second player on the
    same browser never sees it unless they open About.
21. **Q99. A small upkeep shortfall disbands the whole army: change the rule?** (FR-393) Default: no rule change in M12;
    the warning says exactly how many regiments go; credit and debt (rules 06 section 6, `Nation.creditLimit`) come with
    M18. Alternative: pay what the treasury holds and disband only the regiments the rest cannot pay. Objection: the rule
    still wipes out an army for a $100 gap.
22. **Q100. NR-016 says orders wait for End Turn; FR-021 says they apply at once. Which holds?** (FR-440) Default: the
    code's behaviour (FR-021) with full same-turn cancel; NR-016's status says so; queued orders are not planned.
    Alternative: build queued orders (a large engine change, later). Objection: NR-016 was Nathan's own decision (Q9).
23. **Q101. The v1 "World market" dialog cannot be reached in the app: show it or document it?** (FR-439) Default:
    document it as a test-only fixture of the two-islands world. Alternative: a New game option for the v1 world.
    Objection: FR-073, FR-074 and FR-078 describe a screen no player can see.
24. **Q102. Hide the Debug box?** (FR-377) Default: shown only with `?debug` in the URL. Alternative: remove it.
    Objection: bug reports lose the seed unless the reporter knows the switch.
25. **Q103. A baseline moves: keep the old behaviour or re-record the number?** (FR-405) Default: re-record the number
    with its reason, never loosen a check, keep NR-023's margin. Alternative: keep the scripted player's old behaviour
    with special cases. Objection: re-recorded baselines stop proving that nothing else changed.
26. **Q104. Cap offers at the buyers' demand in the engine or on the Board only?** (FR-400) Default: the Board only; the
    engine still accepts a larger offer and returns the surplus. Alternative: the engine refuses it (`limit-reached`).
    Objection: a scripted or future LLM player can still waste a hold.
27. **Q105. Keep FR-455, which changed three v1 turn-1 test cases, or turn it off on the two-islands world?** (FR-159, FR-455)
    FR-159 keeps the v1 tests unchanged; FR-455 (no paper for training while the labour covers what the hubs gather)
    changes the turn-1 run, so twoNations.test.ts's turn-1 trade of `newGame(11)` and its goods-first test, and
    scriptedScore.test.ts's FR-129 plan, were re-recorded. Default taken: keep FR-455 and the re-recorded tests (without
    it FR-125's turn-15 ratio falls to 1.41 to 1.48). Alternative: FR-455 off for the v1 two-islands world; the three
    test cases keep their numbers and FR-125 fails at turn 15. Objection: FR-159 was decision 1, so the v1 tests no
    longer prove that v1 play is unchanged.
28. **Q106. FR-125's no-dead-end check sees a rail being laid: keep it strict, or skip the tip of a built depot's path?**
    (FR-125, NR-022) The check passes on seeds 1 to 4 by timing: on seeds 3 and 4 the depot 20-2's two sections are laid
    on turns 24 and 25, so its tip 19,2 is gone at the turn-25 checkpoint by one turn; a rail one turn later would turn it
    red with nothing wrong. Default taken: keep the check strict (nothing changes). Alternative: a new FR row lets the
    check skip the tip of a path whose depot is built and not yet joined (rail that leads to no depot is still caught).
    Suggestion: the alternative. Objection: it changes a test's check, and a real dead end next to a built depot would
    then pass.
29. **Q107. Should a nation with an army also keep the civilians' cash from training?** (FR-460, FR-454) Default taken:
    no, it trains as before. Measured on the continent (world market, start army): on seed 7, turn 15 four trainings to
    expert left $264, and the engineer's $100 section was dropped by the upkeep floor ($200, FR-213); keeping the
    reserve there too lowered FR-260's start-army scores by up to 55 points (all of it: 1546 -> 1521, 1548 -> 1531,
    1685 -> 1630; only the civilians' share and the upkeep: 1546 -> 1495). Alternative: keep it for every nation.
    Objection: an armed nation's engineer can still wait a turn for a section.

## M12b HUD (NR-034)

Written 2026-11-16 from Nathan's request (NR-034). The design, the pins this change moves, the task split and the risks
are in `docs/specs/hud.md` (the original game's layout: `docs/rules/11-screens.md`). Rows FR-465 to FR-480 are that spec's
HUD-1 to HUD-16 (HUD-n is FR-(464 + n)). Nathan decided two of its cards the same day: Q113 (exactly one Advance Turn,
always on screen) and Q102 (the bug-report group shows without `?debug`); Q108 to Q112 are defaults taken. Rules for every
row: every accessible name, test id and class FR-469 lists stays; the open or closed state of the groups lives in the
HUD's component state, never in the store or the save; no colour literal outside `styles/tokens.css` and
`styles/theme.ts`; jsdom has no layout, so every size claim is checked in e2e; every pinned number this change moves is
re-recorded with its reason and none is loosened (FR-405). The spec names its e2e file `e2e/m13-hud.spec.ts` (written
before the milestone was called M12b); the name is kept.

### M12b decision cards

Q113 is answered; Q108 to Q112 are defaults taken (each is on the progress page with its objection).

- **Q108. Should the top bar have Army and Council buttons?** (FR-466; default taken) The original game has an army panel on the map screen. Here no standing screen exists behind such buttons: the Army screen needs a province (it opens from the map) and the Council screen only replays a meeting from the news. Council information sits in the turn block and the Nation group.
  Options: (a) Default: not now: The bar holds the six dialog buttons, Center, New game, About and Advance Turn. No new behaviour. (b) Army and Council buttons: "Army" opens the Army screen for the capital's province; "Council" opens the last meeting (disabled before turn 10). Small, but new behaviour that needs its own rows.
  Suggestion and default taken: Not now, no Army or Council button. Objection: The original has an army panel on the map screen, so players of the first game may look for it.
- **Q109. New game and About as two buttons or a Game menu, and is a Save button needed?** (FR-466; default taken) The top bar needs a place for New game and About. Autosave runs on every order (FR-357, FR-358) and the "Not saved" alert covers the failure case.
  Options: (a) Default: two plain buttons, no Save button: Names stay for the e2e suite and no extra click. (b) A "Game" menu with New game, About and Save: Fewer buttons in the bar; hides the names from the e2e suite and adds a click.
  Suggestion and default taken: Two plain buttons and no Save button. Objection: New game sits near Advance Turn and could be clicked by mistake. The FR-109 question already guards it.
- **Q110. Should the right pane show a Warehouse summary group?** (FR-474; default taken) Open Warehouse is one click away in the top bar. A group with food, coal and iron would save that click while placing civilians.
  Options: (a) Default: no group: One copy of the stock list, in the Warehouse dialog. (b) A "Warehouse summary" group: Food, coal and iron in the pane; a second copy of the stock list to keep in step.
  Suggestion and default taken: No group now. Objection: Seeing coal and grain while placing civilians saves a dialog round trip. It could come later as one line (food, coal, iron).
- **Q111. Should the war lines stay always in sight, or move into the Nation group?** (FR-471; default taken) A war is rare and must not be missed. The alerts block under Tile Info costs 16 px per war.
  Options: (a) Default: always in sight: In the alerts under Tile Info, with the other warnings. (b) Inside the Nation group: A calmer pane; the war line is hidden until the group is opened.
  Suggestion and default taken: Always in sight. Objection: The brief put the war lines in the Nation group to keep the pane calm.
- **Q112. With no tile selected, should the pane show a placeholder line or nothing?** (FR-470; default taken) FR-315 removed the "No tile selected" hint only to save space; the new pane has the room.
  Options: (a) Default: one line: "No tile selected: click a tile on the map." It tells where Tile Info appears. (b) Nothing: As FR-315 says today.
  Suggestion and default taken: One line, the placeholder stays. Objection: One more line of text that says nothing new to a returning player.
- **Q113. Where does Advance Turn sit?** (FR-466, FR-467; answered by Nathan) The HUD redesign moves the buttons out of the right pane. The spec suggested the top bar's right end; in the original, End Turn is a bar at the bottom of the right column. The other option was a full-width Advance Turn at the pane's bottom (about 40 px).
  Answer: Nathan, 2026-11-16: "one STATIC end turn is enough, just do not force me to scroll to find it at the bottom". So: exactly one Advance Turn button, always on screen, never scrolled away and never at the bottom of a scrolling pane. It is the top bar's right end (FR-466); FR-467 tests it at 1280x720, 800x600 and 390x844 on turn 1 and with a long news list.

  3 news lines) and FR-235 (one scroll area with a fade); Advance Turn is in the bar and never scrolls (card Q113).

  pointer can move over them (WCAG 1.4.13, hoverable); they close when the pointer or the focus leaves the score.

  order added its own price. The engine's charges were not in scope.

- A build id in Details for bug reports (extra E7): not built; the app has no build-time id to show yet.
- The shared `.next` build older than HEAD (extra E6): a process point; the final gate rebuilds it (`npm run build`) and
  the e2e suite runs on its own dev server (`.next-e2e`, FR-444).

## M13 Computer nations that play (T11)

Written 2026-11-16 (spec: `docs/specs/m13-m16.md`, which also holds the lanes, the sim thresholds, the by-eye QA checks and the risks; rows FR-481 to FR-500 are its M13-1 to M13-20, M13-n is FR-(480 + n)). Goal: the computer nation becomes a real opponent on the continent. It raises regiments it can pay and staff, defends and counter-attacks when invaded, keeps its workforce and staffs its mills, competes for the minors' votes with grants and trades steel, lumber and goods with the player; once Arvelia is gone it may go to war with the player, for one province, and then offers peace (cards Q114 and Q115). Every rule reads only what a human could see and acts only through orders. M12 already fixed the steady campaign on a minor (FR-458); FR-487 only pins it over seeds. Every rule acts only on a world with provinces (or in "partners"), so the v1 two-islands baselines and FR-125's margin do not move (FR-498). Sim thresholds are written before the build and never loosened; a row whose threshold cannot be met parks as blocked with its measured numbers. Where a planned row says "will replace", "will change" or "will narrow" an older row, that row stays in force until the new row is built; the task that builds it marks the old row `replaced by`. Status: planned.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-481 | The sim harness (T11; AISIM-01 to -04; promotes the audit's `.gate/audit/ai-sim` tools into the suite): `tests/engine/sim/harness.ts` (new) SHALL play a continent game in "partners" for up to 40 turns as the store does (the human's orders one by one with `tryApplyOrder`, then `computerOrders` and `resolveTurn`), with one of the human policies of `tests/engine/sim/humans.ts` (new) for nation 1: `idle` (End Turn only), `scripted` (`scriptedPlayer`), `aggressor` (the audit's war human: at turn 4 the test setup gives it Arvelia and 6 Cuirassiers + 4 Regulars in its capital province, it declares war on Valdoria on turn 5, then each turn plays the scripted player and recruits up to 2 of each regiment it can) and `trader` (idle, plus each turn it offers 1 steel and 1 lumber that Valdoria bid for last turn and bids for 1 clothing that Valdoria offered last turn, while it has the stock and cash). Per turn it SHALL record: wars declared (by, on, turn), peace made, provinces held, battles (attacker, defender, result), regiments recruited, healthy workers by grade, labour, building capacity, treasury, holds used, Council votes, units traded between Great Powers, `order-rejected` events, `invariantViolations` and the time of `computerOrders`. Verify: `tests/engine/sim/harness.test.ts`: before M13's AI rows, the idle game on seed 1 gives Arvelia taken on turn 17, Valdoria 5 votes to 4 at turn 30, 0 units traded between Great Powers and 4 healthy Valdoria workers at turn 40 (the numbers in section 0). ### M13 by-eye QA fix round (2026-11-17)

Two QA tracks (A and B) played the built app (`.gate/m13/qa/`, scripts in `.gate/audit/qa-m13-A/` and `qa-m13-B/`). They failed
QA checks 2, 3 and 4 and listed 10 extras. Each was checked in the code or with a sim repro first (`.gate/m13/final/`). One was a
defect of the computer's rule and is fixed by the row below; the others are listed under it with what was found.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-541 | The computer keeps its bid while the person answers it (by-eye QA track A extra 2 and track B extra 1; card Q118 (a); adds to FR-493 (a) and FR-491): in "partners", the trade phase SHALL move last turn's public orders (`market.lastOrders`) to `market.ordersTwoTurnsAgo` (optional, absent reads as empty, `SAVE_VERSION` stays 1), and the scripted Great Power SHALL place its FR-493 (a) bid for a commodity before its mills' inputs also when it bid for it last turn and another Great Power it may trade with offered it two turns ago (it answered that offer last turn; the other one sees that answer on its Board now and may be answering it). Why: under Q118 (a) a person may offer only after the computer's bid of last turn, and the computer bid first only after the person's offer of last turn, so a person who answered the Board offered on turns 2, 4, 6 and the computer bid on turns 3, 5, 7: 0 of 145 offers sold in 24 games. Verify: unit `tests/engine/scriptedTrade.test.ts`, `tests/engine/partnerMarket.test.ts`; sim `tests/engine/sim/m13Acceptance.test.ts` with a `follower` human (each turn it offers 1 lumber when the Board says Valdoria bid for lumber last turn, nothing else): it sells at least 1 lumber and at least half of its lumber offers in at least 18 of 24 games (before: 0 of 24). Size: S. | tested (tests/engine/scriptedTrade.test.ts, 3 tests "FR-541 ...": the kept bid in `offeredLastTurn`, before the timber bid with 2 holds left, and End Turn on continent seed 1 turns 1 to 8: offered on 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, sold on 4 to 8 (before: offered on 2, 4, 6, 8, sold never); tests/engine/partnerMarket.test.ts, 2 tests: the record moves each trade phase, a state or save without it reads as empty, "world" writes none; each red before the fix (.gate/m13/final/red-541-unit.txt, `Tests  5 failed \| 112 passed (117)`); tests/engine/sim/m13Acceptance.test.ts FR-541 follower 24 of 24 games (min 8, median 9, max 10 lumber sold; before 0 of 24, .gate/m13/final/red-541-accept.txt). Idle, aggressor and both-scripted games did not move (no person offers lumber or steel there; the acceptance rows and the scripted report are the same line for line before and after); the trader's units traded between the Great Powers rose from min 5, median 6, max 9 to min 8, median 9, max 13. Seen in the built app (Chromium, `next start`, a new game, 1 lumber offered on each turn the Board's Lumber row was open, .gate/m13/final/follow-app.out): offered on turns 2 and 4 to 10, sold on 4 to 10, `offers 8, sold 7; errors []`, News "Sold 1 Lumber to Valdoria for $290." M13 review (2026-11-20): after the FR-493 last-hold fix, the follower lumber check passes in 24 of 24 games. |

Findings at the 2026-11-17 QA checkpoint, before the FR-539 and FR-542 close-out:
- QA checks 2 and 3 (no grant, 0 Other Minor Nations votes at the turn-30 Council): reproduced at that checkpoint. FR-542 later replaced the turn-30 acceptance target.
  The cause in the QA's save: Valdoria holds no embassy in any minor; a grant needs one (FR-162), the embassy costs $5,000 above
  its cash reserve, and its treasury was $3,904 when Sundmark reached 35 points. The close-out implemented Q117 option (b).
- QA check 4 (no steel line on the Board, the steel Offer never open): reproduced at that checkpoint, before the FR-539 standing bid. The lumber
  version of the check works in the app (sold to Valdoria on turns 8 to 14 in track A, 8 to 11 in track B).
- Track B's hypothesis "the 4 holds are used first": confirmed in `economy.ts` `planTrade`: the offers and the raw-input bids fill the
  holds before the FR-493 (a) bids, unless the other Great Power answered (FR-493, FR-541). A bid placed first in every game is
  FR-539's standing bid, which moved an M12 guard (FR-539's status).
- Clothing and furniture bids toward Valdoria never fill (tracks A and B, 0 of 41 and 0 of 28): reproduced in the code. The
  settlement fills the minors' demand from Valdoria's offer (FR-186 step 2) before another Great Power's bid (step 3), and Valdoria
  makes 1 to 2 of each a turn, fewer than the minors want. Not changed: FR-492 keeps the settlement, and serving a Great Power's bid
  in step 2 would move every "partners" pin. The Board's Bid line says a bid is refunded. Decision for Nathan (fix round decision 3).
- Timber offers to Valdoria do not sell (track B, 0 of 6): the same order (step 1 before step 3): Valdoria's timber bid is sized to
  what the minors offer, and the minors fill it first. Not changed, same decision.
- Lumber offers made on the Board's cue are often unsold (tracks A and B): fixed by FR-541; the first answer still finds no bid (the
  one-turn lag of card Q118 (a)).
- Arvelia is taken 2 to 3 turns later than the spec's QA check 1 says (turns 16 to 18): not a defect. The check was written from
  the numbers before M13; M13's migrants and recruits slow the campaign (FR-487's status: war recorded on turns 17 to 18, the
  capital province on 18 to 20, seen in the app one turn later). FR-487's threshold (by turn 25 and 30) holds in 24 of 24.
- After an accepted peace Valdoria declares again 10 turns later and takes a second province (track A, game a1): by design. FR-488
  allows a new war 10 turns after a peace, and QA check 8 asks only for 10 quiet turns; FR-489's sim covers the refused peace.
  Decision for Nathan: a longer truce, or no second war on a player who accepted peace.
- Valdoria's first recruit news comes one turn after the war starts (track B): by the game's rule. A recruit takes its armaments
  from the warehouse when it is ordered (FR-200, the same for the person); at peace the computer keeps no armaments, so on the
  first threatened turn its run makes them and it recruits on the next turn. FR-482's words "the armaments its run makes this
  turn" mean that (its status). The line "1 Cuirassiers recruited." is the game's wording for every nation since M8 (FR-262,
  `e2e/m8-armies.spec.ts`); not changed.
- Process note (track A): its server was stopped and its files are git-ignored; nothing to do.

### M13 decision cards

Defaults taken; each goes on the progress page with its objection. A card marked "Needed before" has a default in force, but the work it decides is only finished once Nathan has answered.

- **Q114. Should the computer go to war with the player?** (FR-488; replaces Q35's "never with a Great Power") After Arvelia falls (turn 16 or 17 in every game), the computer has nothing left to do for 23 turns. The rules report that the AI declares war when the military balance is lopsided (rules 10 section 3).
  Options: (a) never, as today: an idle player is never threatened; (b) from turn 20, when its field army is twice the defence of our weakest border province, with limited aims (Q115); (c) like (b), but also earlier and at random, by a "personality".
  Suggestion: Option (b). Objection: a new or idle player loses a province around turn 25 in most games, and many continent pins move. Some testers will call a punished idle player unfair. The FR-495 warning is the answer to that.
  **Needed before:** Before the M13 military lane (FR-488 and FR-489) is finished and merged: it decides whether the computer may go to war with the player at all, and it changes every idle continent game and many pins. If nobody has answered by then, the night shift builds the suggestion and records it.
- **Q115. What does the computer want from a war with the player?** (FR-489)
  Options: (a) one province, then peace offers every 6 turns; (b) fight on while the estimate allows, which can take the capital and end the game by conquest; (c) take everything except the capital province.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: it is predictable, and an expert player can bait it, for example by leaving a province weak on purpose. The Imp1 AI re-declares when it thinks the player took too much.
  **Needed before:** Before the M13 military lane (FR-489) is finished and merged, together with Q114. Same default rule.
- **Q116. Recruit workers: only to replace losses, or also to staff the mills?** (FR-485, FR-486; NR-028 against NR-023) NR-028 says the computer recruits to use its capacity. M4.4 measured that this lowers its score by about 6%, and FR-125's thinnest margin is 58.5 points (seed 3, turn 25: 903 against 844.5 needed), so a 6% loss would leave about 4.
  Options: (a) FR-485 only: replace the workers its regiments took; (b) (a) plus FR-486, on worlds with provinces only; (c) both rules on both worlds: FR-125 would most likely fail.
  Suggestion: Option (b). Objection: the scripted player then has two rule sets, and the v1 world no longer shows how the continent AI plays.
- **Q117. How does the computer compete for minors' votes?** (FR-490)
  Options: (a) $1,000 grants in the 4 turns before each Council, to the minor where it is between 40 and 49 points and not behind the player; (b) also an embassy ($5,000) and a pact (+10 points); (c) none.
  Suggestion (before the close round): Option (a). Objection: grants cost its treasury, about 30 to 80 score points a game. The player may see this as buying votes, although Imp1 allows the same.
  Default taken (M13 close round, 2026-11-17, running alone; nobody had answered): option (b), the embassy and the pact, built as FR-542: the embassy from 25 relation points (the start's 30 qualifies), the minor with the most points first, never one on its border (the campaign's next target: a war on a pact partner costs 20 points with every country), not for the first meeting, its floor the upkeep reserve, and the campaign recruits and the training keep its $5,000 while it is wanted; the pact the turn after; the campaign spares a pact partner. Measured (idle, seeds 1 to 24): an embassy and a pact before the turn-30 meeting in 21 of 24 games, but 0 Other Minor Nations votes at the turn-30 meeting (option (a) alone: 0 of 24, no embassy in any game) and at least 1 at the turn-40 meeting in 18 of 24. FR-490's turn-30 threshold is replaced by FR-542; card Q139 asks what to change for turn 30.
  Objection: Valdoria pays $5,000 for the embassy and up to $4,000 in grants, about 70 points of its turn-40 score; the player may see the pact and the grants as buying votes (Imp1 allows the same); and the turn-30 promise of FR-490 is still not kept.
- **Q118. How do the two Great Powers trade with each other?** (FR-491 to FR-494) Imp1 shows offer sheets from other powers during the turn. Here the computer orders after the player, so the player cannot see its orders of this turn.
  Options: (a) last turn's bids and offers are public; a bid toward the rival is always allowed (refunded if unfilled); an offer is allowed only after the rival bid for that commodity last turn; (b) Imp1's offer sheets: the computer plans before the player each turn (a large change to the turn loop); (c) no trade between Great Powers.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: there is a one-turn lag, and a bid can come back refunded. The Board line says so.
- **Q119. What does the computer recruit, and how low may its cash go when invaded?** (FR-482, FR-483; remake numbers)
  Options: (a) the two lists in FR-482 and the upkeep reserve as the floor under a threat; (b) choose by strength per dollar; (c) keep Regulars only and train fewer workers.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: the lists ignore price, and Cuirassiers use the 4 scarce horses (more horses come in M15).
- **Q136. Should the computer place a standing steel and lumber bid toward the player?** (FR-539; M13 review ST-02, by-eye QA check 4) In a real game Valdoria bids no steel toward the player, so the Board's Steel row never invites an offer (0 of 24 sim games, and every QA game). A standing bid (1 steel and 1 lumber a turn while a pair is worth buying) met FR-539 in 24 of 24 games, but it uses 2 of the 4 holds every turn and changed an M12 guard test (`tests/engine/scriptedScore.test.ts`, continent seed 1, turn 6) and the both-scripted race (Valdoria first to Arvelia 6 -> 18 of 24 games).
  Options: (a) the standing bid from turn 1, re-recording that one M12 continent test (FR-498 forbids M13 to do that without your answer); (b) the standing bid from turn 7, after that test's turns; (c) no standing bid: FR-539 stays open and the Steel row lights only when Valdoria has a free hold.
  Suggestion (before the close round): Option (a).
  Default taken (M13 close round, 2026-11-17, running alone; nobody had answered): option (b), narrowed. From turn 7 Valdoria keeps a standing bid of 1 steel (no lumber) toward a Great Power a person plays, while its pair bid has steel; it goes before the bids for its mills' inputs, and its cash floor is the upkeep reserve (an unanswered bid is refunded in the same trade phase). Option (b) over the suggested (a): it meets FR-539 without re-recording M12's pin FR-389 in tests/engine/scriptedScore.test.ts (FR-498), and among the 21 variants measured (.gate/m13/close/variants-table.md) it was the one with the least collateral that met both rows (variant p7su: Valdoria first to Arvelia in the both-scripted report 6 -> 19 of 24, its wars on Testland 10 -> 11, Testland's on Valdoria 1 -> 2, lowest final scores 1117/910 -> 1066/830). As built, the bid is topped up to 1 rather than added as 1 more, which measures 6 -> 14, 10 -> 6, 1 -> 7 and 854/830 (the race is chaotic: one seed, 12, gives the 854). Measured: FR-539 (Board) 24 of 24 (was 0), (sold) 24 of 24 (was 1); every other M13 row holds; the v1 guards did not move. The lumber bid is left out: with it Valdoria's wars on Testland in the both-scripted games went 10 -> 21 of 24 (the same variant with steel only: 10 -> 11), and the Lumber row already lights after the person answers (FR-541). Continent pins re-recorded under FR-499: FR-481 (harness), FR-247 (v2Trade40), FR-274/FR-302 (scriptedWar: Arvelia taken on turn 18, was 19).
  Objection: Valdoria spends 1 of its 4 holds a turn from turn 7 on a bid only a trading person answers (refunded otherwise), and the race for Arvelia moves: in the both-scripted report Valdoria takes it first in 14 of 24 games (was 6), Testland declares war on Valdoria in 7 of 24 (was 1), and Testland's lowest final score falls 1117 -> 854 (seed 12). Alternatives: (a) from turn 1 (re-records FR-389; the review fix round's version met FR-539 in 24 of 24, the close round's variant from turn 1 met (Board) in 11 of 24 only and had 2 no-pay disbands), or (c) remove it and reopen FR-539.
- **Q137. Should a Great Power's bid be served before the minors' demand?** (FR-186, FR-492, FR-493 (b); by-eye QA, both tracks) Valdoria's clothing and furniture go to the minors first (settlement step 2), so a player's bid toward it never fills (0 of 69 bids in the QA games), and a player's timber offer never sells (the minors fill Valdoria's timber bid first, step 1).
  Options: (a) keep the order: the Board says a bid is refunded; (b) serve a Great Power's bid for goods in step 2 together with the minors, in id order; (c) the computer keeps a share of its goods for the other Great Power's bid of last turn.
  Suggestion: Option (a) until M14. Objection: the Board invites bids that never pay, and option (b) moves every "partners" pin.
- **Q138. After the player accepts the computer's peace, may it declare war again?** (FR-488, FR-489; by-eye QA track A) FR-488 allows a new war 10 turns after a peace, so an idle player who accepts the first peace (turn 27) is attacked again on turn 37 and loses a second province by turn 40. QA check 8 asks only for 10 quiet turns.
  Options: (a) as built: a new war 10 turns after any peace; (b) no new war on a player who accepted its peace; (c) a longer truce after an accepted peace, for example 20 turns.
  Suggestion: Option (c). Objection: a player who accepts peace is punished like one who refuses it; with (b) the computer has nothing to do after its first war.
- **Q139. What should change so a minor can vote for the computer at the turn-30 Council?** (FR-490, FR-542; M13 close round) FR-490 asked for 1 Other Minor Nations vote for Valdoria at the turn-30 meeting in 18 of 24 idle games. With the embassy, the pact and the grants (card Q117 (b), FR-542) it gets 0 of 24 at turn 30 and 18 of 24 at turn 40: the best free minor stands at 37 to 48 points at turn 30 and a vote needs 50 (FR-293). Valdoria can afford the $5,000 embassy from about turn 23, the turn it goes to war with an idle player (FR-488), and that war keeps its treasury at the upkeep of every turn left (FR-488, FR-497), so it pays almost no grant before turn 30. 36 rule sets were measured (.gate/m13/close/grants-notes.md).
  Options: (a) keep FR-542 as built: the computer's minor votes come at the turn-40 meeting (18 of 24), not at turn 30 (0 of 24); (b) a minor votes for a nominee from 40 relation points instead of 50 (`COUNCIL.minRelationPoints`, FR-293; a remake number, card Q52): measured 23 of 24 at turn 30 and 24 of 24 at turn 40 with FR-542 (5 of 24 at turn 30 without it); the player's votes change too, and every Council pin moves; (c) the computer waits with its war on the player until after the turn-30 meeting: measured with a wait of 8 turns, 0 of 24 at turn 30 and 39 to 101 regiments disbanded for want of pay (FR-497 requires 0), so it needs a new reserve rule first; (d) a cheaper embassy, for example $2,000 (FR-161 keeps Imp1's $5,000): not measured.
  Suggestion: Option (a) for M13; (b) if the turn-30 vote matters, as a Council rule decided for both sides.
  Objection: with (a) the player sees no minor vote for the computer at turn 30, as in the by-eye QA (checks 2 and 3), and the M13 spec's promise moves to turn 40; with (b) a minor is easier to win for the player too, and the Council can end the game earlier.
  **Needed before:** The M13 pull request is merged: FR-490's turn-30 threshold was the spec's, and FR-542 replaces it with the measured ceiling.

## M14 A game that can end well (T12)

Written 2026-11-16 (spec: `docs/specs/m13-m16.md`; rows FR-501 to FR-510 are its M14-1 to M14-10, M14-n is FR-(500 + n)). Goal: every continent game ends with a clear result that follows the rules. The last Council is a forced vote, so the turn-40 meeting names a winner; with two Great Powers 7 of 12 votes win (card Q121, default taken before implementation: the old goal "a peaceful path to 9 votes" cannot be met on this map, the best peaceful total is 8); the final table counts provinces and army; a conquered province counts only when it is collected. Depends on M13's active rival. Where a planned row says "will replace", "will change" or "will narrow" an older row, that row stays in force until the new row is built; the task that builds it marks the old row `replaced by`. Status: tested locally. FR-501 to FR-510 passed the full gate on claude/m14-endgame.

| Id | Requirement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| FR-501 | Every governor chooses at the last meeting (T12; diplomacy-council-14; rules 01 section 4 [M p19]; will replace FR-308; card Q120, which answers Q53): WHEN the Council meets on the last meeting turn of the game (the highest meeting turn at or below `turnLimitOf(state)`; turn 40 of a 40-turn game) THEN each free minor SHALL vote for the nominee with more relation points with it among those it is not at war with, with no 50-point floor. Equal points go to the nominee with the higher standing (FR-291). A minor at war with both nominees abstains. Provinces of the nominees vote as today. Example (probe of 2026-11-16, idle game seed 1, turn 40): Valdoria 4 own + Arvelia + Sundmark (44 against 30) = 6; Testland 4 + Lorran (29 against 30) + Pellis (28 against 30) = 6. Today the result is 5 to 4 with 3 minors abstaining. Verify: unit `tests/engine/council.test.ts`. Size: S. | tested (council.test.ts). Free minors vote below the floor only at the last scheduled meeting and can abstain at war. |

Example: seed 1 above, 6 to 6: Valdoria wins by standing. Verify: unit `council.test.ts`, `tests/engine/gameOver.test.ts`. Size: S. | tested (council.test.ts, gameOver.test.ts). With three or more it stays more than two thirds (FR-294: 81 of 120). The share is data in `definisions/council.ts`. Extends FR-314 and corrects FR-506 under FR-501 to FR-503. | tested (warehouseAndHud.test.tsx, councilDialog.test.tsx, about.test.tsx). Three abstentions leave a 5–4 win at the last meeting. Browser tests that must play to a particular Council SHALL start from a known seed or an explicit game state that reaches it. Keep the full turn sequence and final-state assertions.

### M14 decision cards

Defaults taken; each goes on the progress page with its objection. A card marked "Needed before" has a default in force, but the work it decides is only finished once Nathan has answered.

- **Q120. Should the last Council force every governor to choose?** (FR-501; answers Q53) The rules say yes (rules 01 section 4).
  Options: (a) yes, by relation points, with no floor; (b) no, as today: most turn-40 meetings end with no winner.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: nearly every game gets a "winner" at turn 40 even when both nations are weak, so the score matters less.
- **Q121. How many votes win with only two Great Powers?** (FR-503; answers Q51)
  Options: (a) more than half (7 of 12) while fewer than 3 Great Powers exist; (b) two thirds (9 of 12), as the manual says: a peaceful win stays impossible here; (c) count each minor as 4 votes (its Imp1 size), so 24 votes and 17 needed.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: it departs from the manual. With Arvelia (from turn 17) and two minors, the computer can win at turn 30.
  **Needed before:** Before M14 builds FR-503. REVIVAL_PLAN's old M14 goal "a peaceful path to 9 votes" cannot be met on this map with two Great Powers (best peaceful total 8 of 12), so it needs rewording whichever way this goes.
- **Q122. Equal votes at the last meeting: who wins?** (FR-502)
  Options: (a) the higher standing; (b) the higher score; (c) nobody: the game ends on points.
  Suggestion: Option (a), because standing already orders the nominees. Objection: standing adds 100 per province, so conquest also decides ties.
- **Q123. How should conquered land count in the score?** (FR-505)
  Options: (a) only collected tiles of conquered provinces count; (b) only collected tiles count anywhere (this also changes home tiles and may move FR-125); (c) no change.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: a conqueror who builds a depot gets the points back, and standing still rewards the province itself.
- **Q124. Should a game with no Council name a result?** (FR-508)
  Options: (a) "Best score: X"; (b) nothing, as FR-300 says today.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: How to play calls the best score the goal, but it is the only result on the test world only.

## M15 People and ships (T13)

Written 2026-11-16 (spec: `docs/specs/m13-m16.md`; rows FR-511 to FR-518 are its M15-1 to M15-8, M15-n is FR-(510 + n)). Goal: a nation can build civilians at the University, merchant ships at the Shipyard, and has a steady supply of horses. Before M15, the starting seven civilians and four cargo holds could not be increased through University or Shipyard orders. Living minors (supply, demand and prices that move) are left out of M15 (card Q128). Where a planned row says "will replace", "will change" or "will narrow" an older row, that row stays in force until the new row is built; the task that builds it marks the old row `replaced by`. Status: all eight M15 rows passed Task 07 on 2026-11-20. Task 09 measured the original M14 diplomat and voluntary minor votes with legal raw-material bids. Task 10 records the owner-approved thresholds in FR-545 and FR-546.

Task 09 restores the peaceful test player from 79a94b2. Q140 confines advance material reservation to the computer. The original diplomat wins 15/24 and the idle fleet reaches eight holds by turn 25 in 24/24. FR-516 still requires 18 qualifying fleets. FR-545 records the accepted peaceful win threshold.

### M15 decision cards

Task 07 verification (2026-11-20): each FR has a recorded failing run in `.gate/codex/07/`.
Start M15 browser games from a fixed seed using `e2e/seededGame.ts`. Keep the FR-125 margin, FR-464
two-islands replay and FR-497 planning-time limit of 100 ms. Each changed continent pin records its
old and new value and the gameplay reason. The five decision defaults below remain open for tuning.

Q125 to Q129 all use option (a). Each card remains open for tuning. The journal records the objections.

- **Q125. What does each civilian cost at the University?** (FR-511) The rules give 1 expert, 2 paper and $500 to $1,500 (Prospector $500, Miner $1,500 seen).
  Options: (a) Prospector $500, Miner $1,500, the others $1,000; (b) all $1,000; (c) all $500.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: a guessed price; an Engineer is the most valuable civilian here and may be too cheap.
- **Q126. How do ships and the computer's fleet start?** (FR-514, FR-516)
  Options: (a) the 4 start holds become one Indiaman; the computer builds Indiamen when its holds are full on 3 of 4 turns; (b) the computer starts with 6 to 8 holds, as observed in Imp1; (c) the computer builds no ships.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: the computer's rule acts only on the continent (to keep FR-125), and lumber spent on ships is lumber not spent on mills.
- **Q127. Where do the Horse Ranch tiles go?** (FR-517)
  Options: (a) one open-range tile per Great Power within reach of the capital, and one in Arvelia; (b) only in minors (horses must be bought); (c) none: horses only by trade.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: it changes the hand-drawn map, so every continent tile-count pin moves.
- **Q128. Living minors (supply, demand and prices that move): M15 or later?** (no row yet: M15 leaves them out; MKT-09, MKT-10, RULES-GAP-13)
  Options: (a) a separate M15b after M16; (b) inside M15 (it would need about 2 more hours); (c) in M18.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: trade stays narrow (3 goods with buyers) for one more milestone.
- **Q129. Should the computer build civilians in M15?** (FR-518)
  Options: (a) no, measure it first (it has one Engineer for all its rail); (b) a second Engineer when it has an expert and $1,000 beyond its reserve.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: the player gets a tool the computer does not use, which is an advantage for the player.

## M16 part 1 and M16b — Technology (T14)

M16 part 1 covers FR-519 through FR-522, FR-524, FR-525 and the Technology dialog and Clipper path of FR-526. A version-1 save must replay to its full saved game state (FR-100, FR-520). On turn 3 a loaded game shows the same four inventions as a fresh game (FR-521). The two-islands world market supplies no oil for the refinery until an oil source is implemented (FR-525).

M16b is parked. On main (`9bd5a91`) the computer buys no technology. The resource gates and buyer are on `claude/m16b-technology` (`95634c6`). The buyer-only experiment is on `claude/m16b-buyer` (`49406e9`). Its proposed value rule, FR-547 (card Q143), buys for a planned use within three turns. Measure technology timing and value in the original game before tuning either branch. The earlier `f8470ba` measurements are in `.gate/codex/11/balance.md`.

Both parts use the Q130–Q135 defaults, the configured game length (NR-035), fixed seeds for new browser games, the frozen human policies and the FR-125, FR-464, FR-516, FR-542 (i), FR-545 and FR-546 guards.

### M16 decision cards

Task 11 authorizes the suggested defaults for Q130 to Q135. The cards remain open for later tuning. The journal and verify-and-tune register record each choice and objection.

- **Q130. What game length and which technology years should M16 use?** (FR-521; RULES-GAP-01 already said "the game length must be decided together with the tech tree") The game ends in Winter 1824. With the rules' real years only 5 technologies can appear (Cotton Gin and the four of 1821 to 1825), and level 2 for farms (Steel and Iron Plows, 1831) would never come.
  Options: (a) compress the technology years into the game: 100 years over the turn limit (40 turns: a new set about every 2 turns); (b) the real years, 40 turns: 5 technologies, no level-2 farms; (c) a longer default game (for example 120 turns, 1815 to 1845) with the real years.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: the calendar says 1820 while Internal Combustion is for sale, and the late prices ($40,000 to $150,000) are out of reach in 40 turns.
  **Needed before:** Before M16 starts. It decides whether M16 has 5 technologies or 28. If nobody has answered by then, the night shift builds option (a) with every number in data, so a change costs one file.
- **Q131. Keep NR-015's three free technologies?** (FR-520)
  Options: (a) keep them (rail on hills, timber levels and the mines' level 3 stay open as today); (b) remove them: rail on hills waits for Compound Steam Engine (turn 9 under (a) of Q130, $7,000).
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: Dynamite (an 1871 technology) owned on turn 1 is not the rules.
- **Q132. Do the start civilians need technology?** (FR-524)
  Options: (a) no, the 7 start civilians stay; (b) remove the Rancher, Forester and Driller until their technology is bought.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: a Rancher on turn 1 without Feed Grasses can do nothing until it is bought, which looks like a bug.
- **Q133. Which technology does the computer buy?** (FR-527)
  Options: (a) the cheapest one that unlocks a level it would order; (b) a fixed list from the rules' "recommended early buys" (Iron Railroad Bridge, Square-Set Timbering); (c) none.
  Suggestion: Option (a). Objection: it ignores ships and military technologies until those milestones exist.
- **Q134. Power Plant, oil and era-2 units: now or later?** (FR-525) The fuel-to-labour rate is not documented, and the map has no oil.
  Options: (a) later (M17 and M18): the Power Plant, oil and era-2 units wait for the sea war and the world pieces; (b) now: build the Power Plant and the era-2 units in M16, on a guessed fuel-to-labour rate.
  Suggestion: Option (a): later (M17 and M18). Objection: Oil Drilling can be bought but does nothing.
- **Q135. What happens to saves made before technologies belong to each nation?** (FR-520)
  Options: (a) migrate them; (b) refuse them with FR-362's message.
  Suggestion: Option (a), since Nathan tests on deployed environments with autosaves. Objection: one migration function to keep until the next version.

Each railed tile draws its own piece, from its centre to the middles of the edges it is linked across (`components/railDrawing.ts`, `map/RailLayer.tsx`): two 0.8 px rails 2.6 px apart in `--color-rail`, 4.8 px sepia ties about 5.6 px apart and a faint wash (new token `--color-rail-tie` #725943, the pack's sleeper colour). ### M17 decision cards

All three decisions are planned here and open for Nathan on the roadmap. Suggestions are provisional.

| Id | Owner decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Q144 | **How much water can a squadron cross in one turn?** (a) Use province approaches. A reachable coast takes one sail Order, followed by a separate station turn. Domestic rail stays on the same landmass. A stationed fleet can also reinforce a friendly shore within its support limit. (b) Map several sea zones. Ships travel by their printed speed across a new zone map. The crossing may take more turns. Suggestion: Use province approaches for the first playable crossing. Objection: Short and long voyages take the same time, and holding one approach can close several ports in that province. The original’s limit for follow-up reinforcements is uncertain. | planned |
| Q145 | Owner revision, 2026-11-22. Resolve hostile squadrons with seeded volleys and hull losses (FR-552). Resolve competing allied attacks in descending ARMS order (FR-560). Both outcomes appear in the news. | implemented locally; original-game tuning remains open |
| Q146 | **When should the computer spend on warships?** (a) Respond to a visible threat. Use existing hulls or fund one Frigate after a hostile squadron is seen off one of the computer’s ports. (b) Prepare an offensive fleet. Divert stocks before a threat to seek island wars. This also changes the opening army and diplomacy. Suggestion: Respond to a visible threat. Measure offensive opportunities before choosing a purchase or invasion policy. Objection: A quiet player can take an island minor without a rival naval race, and a reactive build may arrive too late to save a port. | planned |
